#### Sixth edition of Mangaluru Technovanza on Sept 23

**In short:** Mangaluru Technovanza 2026 will be held at Dr TMA Pai International Convention Centre on September 23 under the Beyond Bengaluru initiative by the Electronics, IT and BT Department and Karnataka Digital Economy Mission, expected to bring 600+ delegates, 150+ startups, 40+ global speakers, 50+ global capability centres and 20+ investors to discuss emerging technologies and the cluster’s role as Silicon Beach of India.

#### Kids make eco-friendly Ganesha idols in workshop

**In short:** An eco-friendly clay Ganesha idol-making workshop at Rotary Bala Bhavan, jointly organised by KSPCB Mangaluru, Canara Bank, New Mangalore Port, A J Hospital, CAMP-CO and others, drew about 125 registered children using more than 1,000 kg of clay amid restrictions on Plaster of Paris idols; KSPCB official Dr Manju Rajanna urged natural-clay idols and immersion only at designated tanks.

#### 14 convicted for assault on Bhaskar Shetty murder accused

**In short:** Mangaluru Fast Track Court convicted 14 persons for the 2017 attempt to murder undertrials Navneeth Shetty and Niranjan Bhat inside district prison during the Bhaskar Shetty murder case, with quantum of punishment to be pronounced on September 16; prosecution alleged the accused assaulted the two, struck Shetty with a steel drum and carrom board, and demanded Rs 5 lakh while threatening them to confess to Bhaskar Shetty’s murder.

#### CAMPCO to expand business operations countrywide

**In short:** CAMPCO president S R Satishchandra told the cooperative’s 52nd annual general body meeting at Adyar Garden that it will expand procurement and marketing nationally through agreements with cooperative societies in various states, approved a 14% dividend for arecanut-growing members, and plans an international arecanut conference in Mangaluru in January 2027 plus an arecanut export unit serving markets including Dubai and New Delhi while procuring jackfruit, cashew, cocoa, rubber and black pepper.

#### Follow-up drug screening: 38 of 50 students test negative

**In short:** A follow-up drug screening on Thursday found 38 of 50 college students who had initially tested positive now negative after counselling, out of 8,310 students tested from 150 colleges this year where 98 had first tested positive. Authorities said more than 75% of re-tested students reported stopping drug use and advised continued monitoring for the remaining 12.

#### Vivek Alva to participate in conference in Russia

**In short:** Alva's Education Foundation Managing Trustee M. Vivek Alva, along with five other subject experts from India, will participate in the Third International Conference on "Regional-Level Cooperation within BRICS Nations" in Petrozavodsk, Republic of Karelia, Russia, from Tuesday to Thursday, themed "Modern Environmental and Nature Management Issues" and organised by the Karelian Research Centre of the Russian Academy of Sciences in association with the Russian National Committee on BRICS Research.

Alva's Education Foundation Managing Trustee M. Vivek Alva, along with five other subject experts from India, will participate in the Third International Conference on "Regional-Level Cooperation within BRICS Nations" in Petrozavodsk, Republic of Karelia, Russia, from Tuesday to Thursday, themed "Modern Environmental and Nature Management Issues" and organised by the Karelian Research Centre of the Russian Academy of Sciences in association with the Russian National Committee on BRICS Research. The Indian delegation includes academics from IISc Bengaluru, NIT-K Surathkal, VTU, Chanakya University, and Angadi Institute of Technology and Management; Mr. Alva will speak on "From Awareness to Action: Community-Based Environmental Management in the Western Ghats," showcasing initiatives including Shobhavana and Harish Bhat Pakshivana—the latter recognised under the UN Act to Restore for Climate Action initiative in 2021—and a major initiative covering 46 villages to be brought together through Hasiru Samvada at Alva's Nudisiri–Virasat on January 6 and 7.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Vivek Alva to participate in conference in Russia
- **Told:** Alva's Education Foundation Managing Trustee M.
- **Purpose:** Alva will speak on "From Awareness to Action: Community-Based Environmental Management in the Western Ghats," showcasing initiatives including Shobhavana and Harish Bhat Pakshivana—the latter recognised under the UN Act to Restore for Climate Action initiative in 2021—and a major initiative covering 46 villages to be brought together through Hasiru Samvada at Alva's Nudisiri–Virasat on January 6 and 7.

**Important points**
- The Indian delegation includes academics from IISc Bengaluru, NIT-K Surathkal, VTU, Chanakya University, and Angadi Institute of Technology and Management.

#### Tulu Wikipedia marks 10 years, now has about 4,000 articles

**In short:** Tulu Wikipedia, promoted by the Karavali Wikimedians User Group, celebrated its 10th anniversary with activities in Mangaluru on Saturday. President Vishwanath Badikana said it now has about 4,000 articles and 35 active editors, up from a little over 1,000 when it went live on August 6, 2016, at WikiConference India 2016 in Chandigarh, after eight years in incubation as the 23rd Indian language edition of Wikipedia.

Tulu Wikipedia, promoted by the Karavali Wikimedians User Group, celebrated its 10th anniversary with activities in Mangaluru on Saturday. President Vishwanath Badikana said it now has about 4,000 articles and 35 active editors, up from a little over 1,000 when it went live on August 6, 2016, at WikiConference India 2016 in Chandigarh, after eight years in incubation as the 23rd Indian language edition of Wikipedia. Editors said the project has documented Tulu Nadu's culture, geography, institutions and personalities, and is now documenting living heritage under 'Wiki Loves Tuluvad's Living and Cultural Heritage', with Kanyapu and Sonada Jogi folk performances marking the celebrations.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Tulu Wikipedia marks 10 years, now has about 4,000 articles
- **Told:** Tulu Wikipedia, promoted by the Karavali Wikimedians User Group, celebrated its 10th anniversary with activities in Mangaluru on Saturday.
- **Purpose:** Editors said the project has documented Tulu Nadu's culture, geography, institutions and personalities, and is now documenting living heritage under 'Wiki Loves Tuluvad's Living and Cultural Heritage', with Kanyapu and Sonada Jogi folk performances marking the celebrations.

**Important points**
- Editors said the project has documented Tulu Nadu's culture, geography, institutions and personalities, and is now documenting living heritage under 'Wiki Loves Tuluvad's Living and Cultural Heritage', with Kanyapu and Sonada Jogi folk performances marking the celebrations.

#### Vande Bharat to Bengaluru should leave in morning, say stakeholders

**In short:** Patrons and associations in coastal Karnataka hope the South Western Railway runtime trial of the Vande Bharat Express between Bengaluru and Mangaluru—leaving Bengaluru in the morning and returning at night—will not set the regular service schedule, saying the train should start from Mangaluru early and return by night for people with official and business work in the State capital. G.

Patrons and associations in coastal Karnataka hope the South Western Railway runtime trial of the Vande Bharat Express between Bengaluru and Mangaluru—leaving Bengaluru in the morning and returning at night—will not set the regular service schedule, saying the train should start from Mangaluru early and return by night for people with official and business work in the State capital. G. Hanumanth Kamath of Pashchima Karavali Railway Yatri Abhivriddhi Samithi and KCCI president P.B. Ahmed Mudassar said a morning departure around 6 a.m. would mirror the popular Mangaluru–Thiruvananthapuram Vande Bharat pattern; Thursday's trial covered Yeshwantpur–Mangaluru Junction in 7.05 hours and the return in 6.49 hours, with SWR sources telling The Hindu the target is to complete both directions within six hours by speeding up sections where authorised sectional speed is 110 kmph.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Vande Bharat to Bengaluru should leave in morning, say stakeholders
- **Told:** Patrons and associations in coastal Karnataka hope the South Western Railway runtime trial of the Vande Bharat Express between Bengaluru and Mangaluru—leaving Bengaluru in the morning and returning at night—will not set the regular service schedule, saying the train should start from Mangaluru early and return by night for people with official and business work in the State capital.
- **Purpose:** would mirror the popular Mangaluru–Thiruvananthapuram Vande Bharat pattern; Thursday's trial covered Yeshwantpur–Mangaluru Junction in 7.05 hours and the return in 6.49 hours, with SWR sources telling The Hindu the target is to complete both directions within six hours by speeding up sections where authorised sectional speed is 110 kmph.

**Important points**
- Hanumanth Kamath of Pashchima Karavali Railway Yatri Abhivriddhi Samithi and KCCI president P.B.
- Ahmed Mudassar said a morning departure around 6 a.m.
- would mirror the popular Mangaluru–Thiruvananthapuram Vande Bharat pattern.
- Thursday's trial covered Yeshwantpur–Mangaluru Junction in 7.05 hours and the return in 6.49 hours, with SWR sources telling The Hindu the target is to complete both directions within six hours by speeding up sections where authorised sectional speed is 110 kmph.
_Sources: The Hindu Mangaluru p.3_

#### NSUI alleges violation of rules in M'luru college poll

**In short:** NSUI alleged Students' Union elections at University College, Mangalore on Friday violated state govt guidelines after ABVP-backed candidates won all 37 posts, saying NSUI learnt only on Tuesday that polls would be held Friday despite approaching the vice-chancellor, district minister and principal to follow govt orders. NSUI Dakshina Kannada convener Suhan Alva claimed the higher education secretary said elections held without following the govt order would not be valid and that the V-C had assured polls would not proceed; NSUI said it would condemn the election and demand it be declared invalid, while ABVP's Suvith Shetty said the poll was peaceful.

NSUI alleged Students' Union elections at University College, Mangalore on Friday violated state govt guidelines after ABVP-backed candidates won all 37 posts, saying NSUI learnt only on Tuesday that polls would be held Friday despite approaching the vice-chancellor, district minister and principal to follow govt orders. NSUI Dakshina Kannada convener Suhan Alva claimed the higher education secretary said elections held without following the govt order would not be valid and that the V-C had assured polls would not proceed; NSUI said it would condemn the election and demand it be declared invalid, while ABVP's Suvith Shetty said the poll was peaceful.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** NSUI alleges violation of rules in M'luru college poll
- **Told:** NSUI alleged Students' Union elections at University College, Mangalore on Friday violated state govt guidelines after ABVP-backed candidates won all 37 posts, saying NSUI learnt only on Tuesday that polls would be held Friday despite approaching the vice-chancellor, district minister and principal to follow govt orders.
- **Purpose:** NSUI Dakshina Kannada convener Suhan Alva claimed the higher education secretary said elections held without following the govt order would not be valid and that the V-C had assured polls would not proceed; NSUI said it would condemn the election and demand it be declared invalid, while ABVP's Suvith Shetty said the poll was peaceful.

**Important points**
- Mangalore on Friday violated state govt guidelines after ABVP-backed candidates won all 37 posts, saying NSUI learnt only on Tuesday that polls would be held Friday despite approaching the vice-chancellor, district minister and principal to follow govt orders. NSUI Dakshina Kannada convener Suhan Alva claimed the higher education secretary said elections held without following the govt order would not be valid and that the V-C had assured polls would not proceed.
- NSUI said it would condemn the election and demand it be declared invalid, while ABVP's Suvith Shetty said the poll was peaceful.
_Sources: Times of India Bengaluru p.6_

#### Need for wider reach of Tulu language stressed

**In short:** Karnataka Tulu Sahitya Academy President Taranath Gatti Kapikad, inaugurating the Karavali Wikimedians User Group decennial celebrations in Mangaluru, said Tulu’s literary and cultural wealth confined largely to Dakshina Kannada and Udupi must reach Indian and international audiences through authentic digital documentation; Dr U B Pavanaja said Tulu Wikipedia has gathered 40,000 articles over 10 years, and Wikipedia CEO Bernadette M Meehan praised open repositories across Wikimedia platforms.

#### Udupi’s locked overbridge leaves pedestrians stranded

**In short:** A foot overbridge near Udupi City Municipal Council, built for patients between Urban Primary Health Centre and the old Mother and Child Hospital, stands locked and unused with rust, weeds and blocked staircases as the hospital site changed; social activist Nityananda Olakadu and MLA Yashpal Suvarna urged repair, redesign or relocation, while Commissioner Mahantesh Hangargi said authorities may shift it as a new overbridge near MGM College is set to begin construction next week under a PPP model.

#### 2 arrested for unauthorised rock blasting

**In short:** Manipal police arrested K Govinda Raj (40) of Inna, Karkala, and Shekhappa Chalavadi alias Chandru (28) from Halekatte, Karkala, for alleged unauthorised rock blasting on 1.54 acres of government land in Kukkipalke, Marne village, Udupi taluk on September 5 without departmental permission, registering a case under BNS Sections 288 and 3(5) and Explosives Act Section 9(b) and seizing a tractor with drilling compressor and a car.

#### Protest over insult to Kharge

**In short:** Former minister Jayaprakash Hegde at a Udupi District Congress black-badge protest near Gandhi statue at Ajjarkadu accused the Uttarakhand BJP government of insulting constitutional values by allegedly ordering purification of the stage where AICC president Mallikarjun Kharge spoke, while former minister Vinay Kumar Sorake said the BJP undermines constitutional principles through untouchability; the protest also opposed an FIR against Rahul Gandhi over his protest against the incident.

#### Ivan seeks fact-finding panel on K’rangan report’s impact

**In short:** MLC Ivan D’Souza urged Karnataka MPs to press the Centre for a fact-finding committee on the Kasturirangan Committee report’s impact before any decision, saying the state rejected draft notifications six times, and announced a stakeholders’ meeting in Dakshina Kannada and Udupi within a week ahead of the September 21–23 legislature special session; he also said CM D K Shivakumar will meet ruling and Opposition MLAs on September 18 on coastal employment proposals including CRZ relaxation and demanded Mangalore University conduct direct students’ council elections per government circular.

#### MLC seeks ₹5,000-crore special package for coastal Karnataka at Cabinet meeting

**In short:** MLC and State Congress working president Manjunath Bhandary said he had sought a ₹5,000-crore special economic package for coastal Karnataka to be included in the September 18 Mangaluru Cabinet meeting agenda to improve infrastructure, fisheries, tourism, and higher education and protect coastal culture, and that Chief Minister D.K. Shivakumar responded positively and asked the Chief Secretary to include prominent demands.

MLC and State Congress working president Manjunath Bhandary said he had sought a ₹5,000-crore special economic package for coastal Karnataka to be included in the September 18 Mangaluru Cabinet meeting agenda to improve infrastructure, fisheries, tourism, and higher education and protect coastal culture, and that Chief Minister D.K. Shivakumar responded positively and asked the Chief Secretary to include prominent demands. His memorandum also sought an advanced skill development centre, a permanent solution to coastal erosion, upgrading the Brahmavara Agriculture/Horticulture Diploma College as an Agricultural University using 342 acres and ₹20 crore infrastructure, upgrading Mangaluru Fisheries College as a fisheries university, establishing a Karnataka Fisheries Council, developing a modern Aqua Park, raising compensation for fishermen's families who die while fishing from ₹5 lakh to ₹10 lakh, completing the urban ecopark at Pilikula, developing a Karavali religious tourism circuit, and building a coastal parallel road from Talapady to Byndoor.

#### Pranavi Urs targets Asian Games medal, eyes LET and LPGA dream

**In short:** Mysuru golfer Pranavi Urs, part of India's women's Asian Games team with Aditi Ashok and Diksha Dagar, said consistent rounds of three or four under and solid driving matter more than chasing low numbers in Japan. The 23-year-old LET player, first woman IGPL winner with a 60, said she will treat the Games like any international event and hopes to win a medal.

#### Zero SIR notices: M'luru voters felicitate BLO

**In short:** Voters at booth No 7 of Dakshina Kannada Zilla Panchayat Higher Primary School in Ullal under Mangaluru constituency recorded 100% clean mapping in SIR, with none of the 782 voters — 398 men and 384 women from Kodi, Dota and surrounding areas — served hearing notices or struck off rolls, and they felicitated booth level officer Amrutha Rahul for door-to-door groundwork guiding forms and documents. Amrutha said preliminary work completed in advance meant no notices were needed, in contrast to the rest of Dakshina Kannada where the revision remains rigorous and tense.

### Karnataka

#### Being a woman, living in a world of fear

**In short:** Slow redressal, legal lacunae and victim shaming belittle sexual harassment trauma, as illustrated by three cases: a Bengaluru analyst spat on near Kengeri bus stop, Ahmedabad journalist Devanshi Joshi facing rape threats after a 2017 YouTube investigation, and a postgraduate student photographed without consent on Namma Metro. Harassment spans real and virtual worlds, and broadly covers improper conduct causing humiliation or distress.

#### 46 answer scripts of BCom students vanish at BU

**In short:** Answer scripts of 46 BCom students in the Modern Banking subject have gone missing at Bangalore University, leaving fourth-semester pupils awaiting results since April 2025 after January exams whose other results were declared in March. The Syndicate decided to declare results based on marks in other subjects and issue notices to the exam custodian, systems engineer and college principal, but members including Dr H J Jaikrishna demanded a detailed inquiry, citing prior missing-script cases involving 97 students between 2013 and 2023 and alleged mismanagement in the evaluation department.

#### Shopping malls do not require trade licence, rules HC

**In short:** A Karnataka High Court division bench held that shopping malls within BBMP jurisdiction do not require trade licences though individual shops inside do, rejecting BBMP’s demand that Island Star Mall Developers renew trade licence fees for a diesel generator set after the firm had paid Rs 10 lakh and renewed until 2013. The bench said section 354 of the Karnataka Municipal Corporations Act requires permission for installation, not annual trade-licence renewal, and that BBMP rendered no inspection service beyond collecting fees while electrical inspector and KSPCB handled certification.

#### Rajanna terms ED raids on Jarkiholi ‘politically motivated’

**In short:** Congress veteran K N Rajanna on Saturday condemned ED raids on properties linked to Public Works Minister Satish Jarkiholi as politically motivated, saying Jarkiholi’s family business has Rs 2,000 crore annual turnover and questioning the significance of seizing Rs 2 crore; he denied reports of quitting Congress over missing an Apex Bank term, said he may or may not contest the next Assembly polls from Madhugiri on a Congress ticket, and deflected questions on CM claims that four to five ministers could face ED raids.

#### Loans for minorities

**In short:** The Karnataka Minorities Development Corporation has invited online applications from eligible minority-community members for 2026-27 loans under schemes including Swavalambi Sarathi, Fast Food Truck/Tailer Kitchen Food Kiosk, women’s self-help group self-employment promotion, Sikh community economic empowerment, Community-Based Training, Direct Loan, Foreign Education Loan and Santhwana, with applications due by September 17; enquiries at 8277799990.

#### Free training in tailoring, fashion designing

**In short:** RRUDSETI, with Sri Dharmasthala Manjunatheshwara Education Trust and Canara Bank, will conduct free 31-day tailoring and fashion designing training for rural unemployed women from September 17; applicants must be aged 18–50, read and write Kannada, and hold BPL and Aadhaar cards, with free food and accommodation and Ministry of Rural Development certificates on completion; contact RUDSET, Arishinakunte, Nelamangala taluk, or 9380162042, 9241482541, 9481506564 or 9113880324.

#### 503 child labourers rescued in raids across Bengaluru

**In short:** Joint teams from Bengaluru City Police, Labour Department and Women and Child Development rescued 503 child labourers during surprise inspections at 5,897 locations on Saturday and registered 18 cases under child-labour and juvenile justice laws, with West Division reporting 84 rescues, East 71 and Central 63; a CCB team separately rescued 45 children from 420 locations and registered nine cases following Chief Minister D K Shivakumar’s direction after visiting Yeshwantpur APMC yard.

#### Govt school hours may be changed to avoid B’luru traffic

**In short:** School Education Minister Madhu Bangarappa said he will discuss revising Bengaluru government school timings after teachers reported needing to leave 1.5 hours early yet spending about an hour in traffic to reach schools running 10.30 am–4.30 pm, while private schools operate 8.45 am–3.45 pm; teachers suggested 8.30 am–3.30 pm and Bangarappa said the government is also considering private unaided school staff for State Best Teachers awards.

#### 90% of kidnapping cases reported in B’luru false

**In short:** Data accessed by DH showed about 90% of kidnapping and abduction cases in Bengaluru between 2022 and 2025 were false, including 91.66% in 2022, 86.18% in 2025 and 47.99% in 2026 till August, largely from elopement complaints families later drop; Commissioner Seemant Kumar Singh said minors who elope must be treated as kidnaps and many cases end false when couples return, contributing to low conviction as victims and complainants turn hostile.

#### Proposed ring roads get ‘wider’ development push

**In short:** The Karnataka government will open a four-km-wide development corridor along proposed ring roads in 167 Tier-2 and Tier-3 cities under its Road Network Grid Plan, treating two km on either side as an Impact Zone permitting residential townships, commercial, logistics, educational and tourism projects among others; Chief Minister D K Shivakumar said a new road grid is being formed for mobility, covering cities including Belagavi, Ballari, Chitradurga, Badami, Gauribidanur, Belthangady and Shikaripura.

#### 'I know who complained to ED': Jarkiholi denies foreign funds

**In short:** PWD minister Satish Jarkiholi said he knows who complained against him to the Enforcement Directorate and has informed CM DK Shivakumar, describing the complainant as having Congress links but being a BJP voter, while denying foreign investments and bribery allegations after ED seized Rs 3.4 crore in cash during Sept 9–10 searches at 21 premises in Bengaluru, Belagavi, Gokak and Kolkata under FEMA over suspected undisclosed overseas investments. BJP state president BY Vijayendra questioned whether Congress internal rivalry over the 2028 chief minister's post lay behind the raids, and opposition leader R Ashoka alleged ruling-party functionaries were complaining against colleagues to investigative agencies.

#### Pvt environmental lab faces probe after min's inspection

**In short:** Bengaluru Analytics and Research Laboratory Pvt Ltd faces scrutiny after forest, ecology and environment minister Ramalinga Reddy and senior KSPCB officials conducted a surprise inspection and found multiple irregularities, including operation without valid KSPCB consent after its establishment consent expired in 2025 despite prior MoEFCC permission stating three analysts would be employed when only one was present. Officials found no proper waste-disposal system and alleged waste was discharged into an open pit.

#### Medical colleges to adopt taluk hospitals

**In short:** The state govt has directed every government and private medical college to adopt one taluk hospital, one Community Health Centre and one Primary Health Centre under a Health Department circular issued Thursday, as part of the chief minister's 100-day programme to deploy gynaecologists, anaesthesiologists, paediatricians and orthopaedic surgeons in rural areas. The plan includes district-level agreements, specialist OPDs with diagnostic and operation-theatre facilities, emergency transport via 108 Arogya Kavach, medicine supply, ASHA pre-registration, rotating consultants and postgraduate students, and continuing medical education on rural health profiles.

The plan includes district-level agreements, specialist OPDs with diagnostic and operation-theatre facilities, emergency transport via 108 Arogya Kavach, medicine supply, ASHA pre-registration, rotating consultants and postgraduate students, and continuing medical education on rural health profiles.

#### Mines amalgamation: NGO files contempt petition

**In short:** Samaj Parivarthana Samudaya filed a Supreme Court contempt petition against chief secretary Shalini Rajneesh and senior officials alleging amalgamation of five cancelled ‘C’ category mine blocks with ‘B’ and ‘A1’ leases and virgin forest in violation of apex court orders, and separately sought acceptance of Justice Sudershan Reddy’s recommendation to limit iron ore production in four Karnataka districts to 20 million tonnes per year for intergenerational equity over 75 years.

#### Can Cabinet panel take school fees’ bull run by the horns?

**In short:** A Cabinet sub-committee on students’ issues includes regulating private-institution fees among its terms of reference, but Karnataka lacks a dedicated fee-regulation committee and courts have struck down rigid fee-fixing, including the 2023 Dharwad bench ruling against section 48 of the Karnataka Education Act for unaided schools, an order the government has appealed. Private school associations accept regulation of fees above 20% excess but reject fee formulas, while parents’ groups cite Tamil Nadu’s judge-headed commission model and Minister Madhu Bangarappa said legal issues must be resolved; CM D K Shivakumar warned institutions charging over Rs 50,000 annually to adopt village government schools or face fee caps.

#### Anti-SIR forum to launch campaign in state from Sept 22

**In short:** Civil society groups opposing Karnataka’s Special Intensive Revision of electoral rolls announced a study of 100 polling booths and protests at Jantar Mantar on October 1–2, with actor Prakash Raj alleging voters marked shifted or absent are forced into Form 6 rather than Form 8 address updates; the forum claimed over 1.08 crore voters were flagged or removed and about 66 lakh shifted voters wrongly categorised, demanding SIR be scrapped, commissioner resignations and legislature discussion during the September 21 special session.

#### Gubbi RFO, camp watcher held in bribery case

#### Protest against limiting 'Vande Mataram' rendition

**In short:** BJP State president B.Y. Vijayendra said on Saturday the party will organise a five-day padayatra across all 224 Assembly constituencies in Karnataka from Tuesday to protest the State government's decision to limit Vande Mataram to two stanzas at official events.

BJP State president B.Y. Vijayendra said on Saturday the party will organise a five-day padayatra across all 224 Assembly constituencies in Karnataka from Tuesday to protest the State government's decision to limit Vande Mataram to two stanzas at official events. Party workers and members of the public will meet Tahsildars and Deputy Commissioners to submit memoranda demanding withdrawal of the order, which Chief Minister D.K. Shivakumar took at a recent Cabinet meeting; Karnataka has directed that only the first two stanzas be sung at State programmes except those attended by the President, Vice-President, Prime Minister or Governor.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Protest against limiting 'Vande Mataram' rendition
- **Told:** BJP State president B.Y.
- **Purpose:** Shivakumar took at a recent Cabinet meeting; Karnataka has directed that only the first two stanzas be sung at State programmes except those attended by the President, Vice-President, Prime Minister or Governor.

**Important points**
- Party workers and members of the public will meet Tahsildars and Deputy Commissioners to submit memoranda demanding withdrawal of the order, which Chief Minister D.K.
- Shivakumar took at a recent Cabinet meeting.
- Karnataka has directed that only the first two stanzas be sung at State programmes except those attended by the President, Vice-President, Prime Minister or Governor.

#### Four student teams, 15 startups win C-CAMP bio entrepreneurship contest

**In short:** Fifteen startups and four student teams won the ninth C-CAMP National Bio Entrepreneurship Competition 2026 from 25 finalists shortlisted from 74 pre-finalists and over 3,200 registrations nationwide, sharing Rs 35 crore-plus in cash and investment for startups and Rs 4 lakh for student teams; winning projects spanned cancer pathology, quantum kidney screening, bone regeneration and affordable insulin pumps among others.

#### K'taka considers bringing back physical marks cards

**In short:** The higher education department is considering revoking its circular that stopped issuing physical marks cards after vice-chancellors at a Karnataka State Higher Education Council meeting with minister Basavaraj Rayareddi flagged student difficulties with DigiLocker-only records, including rejection by foreign universities and corporates. The 2023-24 budget had switched fully to digital marks cards to curb fake certificates; a proposed hybrid system would issue both physical and digital cards with online verification, uniform minimum and maximum printing rates across universities, and a committee to recommend security features within a week.

#### Fewer ATHLETES, same EXPECTATIONS

**In short:** Twenty-three Karnataka sportspersons, most making their Asian Games debut, are part of India’s 500-plus contingent for the 20th Asian Games in Aichi-Nagoya, Japan, from September 19 — down from roughly 37 at Hangzhou four years ago. Medal hopes include pole-vault record holder Sindhushree G, 400m hurdler Yashas P, relay runners Unnathi Aiyappa, Sneha SS and Poovamma MR, Mangaluru shuttler Ayush Shetty, golfers Aditi Ashok and Pranavi Urs, swimmers led by Srihari Nataraj, hockey goalkeeper Mohith HS, and shooter Tilottama Sen; Deputy Chief Minister G Parameshwara announced Rs 5 lakh each for state athletes at the Commonwealth Games and Asiad, atop Rs 35 lakh, Rs 25 lakh and Rs 15 lakh for gold, silver and bronze.

#### Pipers, Grandmasters reach final

**In short:** On a dramatic Saturday in the Global Chess League in Bengaluru, Alpine APL Pipers and Ganges Grandmasters — both on 18 points — advanced to the Sunday final after the top three teams chasing a final berth all lost to bottom-placed sides; FYERS American Gambits and CheQ Mumba Masters (15 points each) will play for third place. Gambits needed at least nine game points against Masters but fell 11-6 after Nihal Sarin squandered a winning position, while Magnus Carlsen’s Pipers lost 11-6 to Triveni Continental Kings and Grandmasters lost 9-7 to PBG Alaskan Knights despite needing only one game point to qualify.

#### Srinath's delicate balancing act of coaching two sides

**In short:** GM Srinath Narayanan, coach of FYERS American Gambits at the Bengaluru Global Chess League, will switch to India’s national team at the Chess Olympiad in Samarkand from September 16, working with Arjun Erigaisi, R Praggnanandhaa, Nihal Sarin, D Gukesh and Vidit Gujrathi after coaching an international Gambits squad that includes Uzbek icon Javokhir Sindarov. Srinath said he and Sindarov maintain clear boundaries on personal chess matters while functioning as one unit, ahead of Sindarov leading Uzbekistan and later facing Gukesh in the World Chess Championship.

#### Rusty SC Bengaluru start on winning note

**In short:** Former champions SC Bengaluru opened their KSFA Super Division campaign with a 2-0 win over ASC FC at the BFS on Saturday, Ngmring Kampuinamei scoring in the first minute and Kangbi John in the 88th. **In short:** Kerala winger Ashique Kuruniyan, 29, says returning to Bengaluru FC after injury-hit years at Mohun Bagan — including an ACL tear playing for India against Iraq in September 2023 — has restored his joy under coach Pep Munoz ahead of friendlies against Panama, Brazil and Uruguay. #### The gallery that grew with the city

**In short:** Deepa Natarajan Lobo recounts ophthalmologist Dr Premilla Baid founding Gallery Sumukha in Bengaluru in 1996 after charity shows and early Chittrovanu Mazumdar and Kolkata artist exhibitions, moving from a family outhouse to Alliance Française and Wilson Garden in 1999. Thirty years on, Sumukha hosted artists from K G Subramanyan and Gulammohammed Sheikh to younger names, Peter Nagy-curated shows, and international fairs, with Dr Premilla urging welcoming spaces where visitors discover art without pressure to buy.

#### HC orders SIT probe into 16-year-old accident case

**In short:** The Karnataka High Court Dharwad bench ordered an SIT probe into a 2009 road accident case, suspecting manipulation to claim insurance compensation, while dismissing appellant Giridhar Neelakantha Pai's appeal and imposing Rs 10,000 costs. The court noted Giridhar lacked a valid licence, inconsistencies in hospital records, and possible collusion among complainants to defraud the insurer.

#### Bengaluru girl missing for over seven months traced to Kanakapura using Aadhaar OTP

**In short:** An 18-year-old second-year PU student missing from Bengaluru's Vidyaranyapura since January 31 was traced to Kanakapura taluk after CID investigators linked her Aadhaar use to an OTP on her husband's phone. The girl, found married and pregnant, was handed to her parents after a habeas corpus petition and High Court proceedings.

#### Actor Prakash Raj announces campaign across 10 districts in Karnataka

**In short:** Prakash Raj said he will conduct a social audit of the Special Intensive Revision of electoral rolls across 10 districts from September 20 to 30, visiting booths to compare EC records with deleted names and gathering evidence from affected voters. #### DyCM warns caste census could be a 'meaningless' count

**In short:** Deputy CM G Parameshwara warned that an open-ended 2027 Census caste methodology could fragment communities into thousands of duplicate entries and undermine social justice data. #### Over 250 clips on mobile, pendrive seized from Prajwal

**In short:** Forensic analysis of the mobile phone and pen drive seized from the prison cell where former MP Prajwal Revanna was lodged during a CCB raid found over 147 video clips on the phone and more than 100 videos on the pen drive, reportedly including porn clips, OTT series and movies. Revanna has reportedly told investigators that he used the phone mainly for making calls, admitted contacting his mother through a phone used by one of her maids rather than calling her directly, and claimed the phone was circulated among several prisoners on a rotational basis so he did not know who downloaded the material.

Forensic analysis of the mobile phone and pen drive seized from the prison cell where former MP Prajwal Revanna was lodged during a CCB raid found over 147 video clips on the phone and more than 100 videos on the pen drive, reportedly including porn clips, OTT series and movies. Revanna has reportedly told investigators that he used the phone mainly for making calls, admitted contacting his mother through a phone used by one of her maids rather than calling her directly, and claimed the phone was circulated among several prisoners on a rotational basis so he did not know who downloaded the material. Police are scrutinising call records and are likely to issue notices to persons contacted from the device; Revanna is said to have used the mobile in jail for regular and video calls and reportedly paid around ₹1 lakh to get the phone.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Over 250 clips on mobile, pendrive seized from Prajwal
- **Told:** Forensic analysis of the mobile phone and pen drive seized from the prison cell where former MP Prajwal Revanna was lodged during a CCB raid found over 147 video clips on the phone and more than 100 videos on the pen drive, reportedly including porn clips, OTT series and movies.
- **Purpose:** Police are scrutinising call records and are likely to issue notices to persons contacted from the device; Revanna is said to have used the mobile in jail for regular and video calls and reportedly paid around ₹1 lakh to get the phone.

**Important points**

_Sources: The Hindu Mangaluru p.1_

BJP State president B.Y. Vijayendra and Leader of Opposition R.

Public Works Minister Sathish Jarkiholi, recently raided by the Enforcement Directorate (ED), said on Saturday that he knew who had complained against him and had asked the Chief Minister to look into it, adding that the complainant "had relations with the Congress, but was a BJP voter." Ashok seized on the remark, with Mr. Ashok demanding that Mr. Jarkiholi disclose the Congress leader who "backstabbed" him; Mr. Jarkiholi said he would contest ED suspicions legally and maintained offshore investments were planned but not yet made, while the ED on Friday claimed the family invested in three mining companies in Congo and Zambia and raided Bharat Vanijya Eastern Private Ltd. over alleged PWD tender bribes.

#### Sugar price spike revives farmers' demand for jaggery-based ethanol units

**In short:** A surge in sugar prices ahead of the festive season — Gowri-Ganesha, Dasara and Deepavali — amid reports of sugarcane diversion for ethanol has revived Karnataka farmers' demand that jaggery and grain-based units be allowed to produce ethanol. State Sugarcane Growers' Association president Kurubur Shanthakumar said ethanol procurement at around ₹65 a litre could substantially raise growers' incomes, noting Karnataka produces about 600 lakh tonnes of sugarcane annually but only 30 to 40 lakh tonnes go to some 15,000 jaggery units.

A surge in sugar prices ahead of the festive season — Gowri-Ganesha, Dasara and Deepavali — amid reports of sugarcane diversion for ethanol has revived Karnataka farmers' demand that jaggery and grain-based units be allowed to produce ethanol. State Sugarcane Growers' Association president Kurubur Shanthakumar said ethanol procurement at around ₹65 a litre could substantially raise growers' incomes, noting Karnataka produces about 600 lakh tonnes of sugarcane annually but only 30 to 40 lakh tonnes go to some 15,000 jaggery units. Speakers at a recent Bengaluru seminar cited maize prices crossing ₹3,200 a quintal from below ₹2,800 before ethanol expansion, and said Karnataka's 56 ethanol units rank third nationally with about 135 crore litres produced annually.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Sugar price spike revives farmers' demand for jaggery-based ethanol units
- **Told:** A surge in sugar prices ahead of the festive season — Gowri-Ganesha, Dasara and Deepavali — amid reports of sugarcane diversion for ethanol has revived Karnataka farmers' demand that jaggery and grain-based units be allowed to produce ethanol.
- **Purpose:** Speakers at a recent Bengaluru seminar cited maize prices crossing ₹3,200 a quintal from below ₹2,800 before ethanol expansion, and said Karnataka's 56 ethanol units rank third nationally with about 135 crore litres produced annually.

**Important points**
- Speakers at a recent Bengaluru seminar cited maize prices crossing ₹3,200 a quintal from below ₹2,800 before ethanol expansion, and said Karnataka's 56 ethanol units rank third nationally with about 135 crore litres produced annually.
_Sources: The Hindu Mangaluru p.1; The Hindu Mangaluru p.4_

#### CM to hear people from regions affected by Kasturirangan report

**In short:** Chief Minister D.K. Shivakumar, who will chair a Cabinet meeting at Praja Soudha in Mangaluru from 10 a.m.

Chief Minister D.K. Shivakumar, who will chair a Cabinet meeting at Praja Soudha in Mangaluru from 10 a.m. on September 18, will hold public meetings in Udupi, Shivamogga, and Chikkamagaluru districts on September 18 and 19 to hear from people in areas that could be affected by the Kasturirangan Committee report, travelling by helicopter to Hebri (3 p.m. on September 18), Tirthahalli (10 a.m. on September 19), and Balehonnur (1 p.m. on September 19) before returning to Bengaluru that evening. Meanwhile, MLC Ivan D'Souza said he will interact with people affected by the report ahead of the September 21–23 special legislature session, noting that although the State government has rejected the report eight times, the Union government has been pressuring southern states to accept it and that as many as seven districts would be impacted.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** CM to hear people from regions affected by Kasturirangan report
- **Told:** Chief Minister D.K.
- **Purpose:** Meanwhile, MLC Ivan D'Souza said he will interact with people affected by the report ahead of the September 21–23 special legislature session, noting that although the State government has rejected the report eight times, the Union government has been pressuring southern states to accept it and that as many as seven districts would be impacted.

**Important points**
- on September 18, will hold public meetings in Udupi.

#### Worker attacked

**In short:** The Whitefield police have booked a woman who allegedly assaulted a civic worker with a stone after he asked her not to throw a used tea cup on the roadside on Pattandur Agrahara Main Road on Friday. The incident occurred on Kuvempu Road, where the woman, identified as Vaidehi, a private firm employee, was with her male friend after having tea; when the civic worker asked them to put the cup in a dustbin instead of littering, Vaidehi allegedly abused him and hurled a stone at him, with witnesses claiming she appeared to be under the influence of alcohol, after which a police team reached the spot and detained Vaidehi and her friend.

#### Fake doctors will face strict legal action, DHOs and THOs could be held accountable: Health Minister

**In short:** Health and Family Welfare Minister U.T. Khader said in Dharwad on Saturday that stringent legal action would be taken against fake doctors and that District Health Officers and Taluk Health Officers could also be held accountable if such practitioners were found operating in their jurisdictions, with an official order to be issued shortly.

Health and Family Welfare Minister U.T. Khader said in Dharwad on Saturday that stringent legal action would be taken against fake doctors and that District Health Officers and Taluk Health Officers could also be held accountable if such practitioners were found operating in their jurisdictions, with an official order to be issued shortly. Speaking to presspersons, he said if a fake doctor is found during an inspection, the concerned DHO will be held responsible; he also said the government had prohibited sale of paan masala and gutka containing tobacco and nicotine, was conducting inspections on food adulteration and urging consumers to report poor hygiene via a QR code system, and described Enforcement Directorate action against Minister Satish Jarkiholi as part of "targeted politics," alleging a large proportion of ED cases were aimed at Congress leaders while similar action was not taken against BJP leaders.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Fake doctors will face strict legal action, DHOs and THOs could be held accountable: Health Minister
- **Told:** Health and Family Welfare Minister U.T.
- **Purpose:** Speaking to presspersons, he said if a fake doctor is found during an inspection, the concerned DHO will be held responsible; he also said the government had prohibited sale of paan masala and gutka containing tobacco and nicotine, was conducting inspections on food adulteration and urging consumers to report poor hygiene via a QR code system, and described Enforcement Directorate action against Minister Satish Jarkiholi as part of "targeted politics," alleging a large proportion of ED cases were aimed at Congress leaders while similar action was not taken against BJP leaders.

**Important points**
- Speaking to presspersons, he said if a fake doctor is found during an inspection, the concerned DHO will be held responsible.
- he also said the government had prohibited sale of paan masala and gutka containing tobacco and nicotine, was conducting inspections on food adulteration and urging consumers to report poor hygiene via a QR code system, and described Enforcement Directorate action against Minister Satish Jarkiholi as part of "targeted politics," alleging a large proportion of ED cases were aimed at Congress leaders while similar action was not taken against BJP leaders.
_Sources: The Hindu Mangaluru p.3_

#### Opposition seeks Minister's resignation amid ED probe

**In short:** Ahead of a three-day special Legislative Assembly session from September 21 on drought, farmers' distress and the Kasturirangan report, the BJP and JD(S) are set to intensify their campaign for Public Works Minister Satish Jarkiholi's resignation after ED searches at 21 premises linked to him, his family and associates. The ED reported seizure of ₹3.3 crore in unaccounted cash and alleged bribery in PWD contract awards and undisclosed overseas investments; Leader of Opposition R.

Ahead of a three-day special Legislative Assembly session from September 21 on drought, farmers' distress and the Kasturirangan report, the BJP and JD(S) are set to intensify their campaign for Public Works Minister Satish Jarkiholi's resignation after ED searches at 21 premises linked to him, his family and associates. The ED reported seizure of ₹3.3 crore in unaccounted cash and alleged bribery in PWD contract awards and undisclosed overseas investments; Leader of Opposition R. Ashok demanded Mr. Jarkiholi's immediate removal until the probe is complete, citing a strategy similar to that used against former Minister B. Nagendra in the Valmiki corporation case — both ministers belong to the Nayaka Scheduled Tribe community.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Opposition seeks Minister's resignation amid ED probe
- **Told:** Ahead of a three-day special Legislative Assembly session from September 21 on drought, farmers' distress and the Kasturirangan report, the BJP and JD(S) are set to intensify their campaign for Public Works Minister Satish Jarkiholi's resignation after ED searches at 21 premises linked to him, his family and associates.
- **Purpose:** Nagendra in the Valmiki corporation case — both ministers belong to the Nayaka Scheduled Tribe community.

**Important points**
- Ashok demanded Mr.
- Jarkiholi's immediate removal until the probe is complete, citing a strategy similar to that used against former Minister B.
- Nagendra in the Valmiki corporation case — both ministers belong to the Nayaka Scheduled Tribe community.

#### Solar power to reach 1,900 anganwadis

**In short:** The Department of Women and Child Development has partnered with the SELCO Foundation to provide solar power to 1,900 anganwadi centres across Karnataka, focusing on remote and underserved areas, under a Memorandum of Understanding signed by WCD Director Mahesh Babu and SELCO Foundation Director Huda Jaffer. The initiative, estimated to cost about ₹16 crore, aims to improve basic facilities for early childhood care, preschool education, nutrition and health services, with training for anganwadi staff on solar system use and the Soura e-Mitra application to report faults and access technical support.

#### KITU flags deletion of women's night-shift safeguards; Labour Department says OSH Code covers them

**In short:** The Karnataka State IT/ITeS Employees' Union has condemned the omission of several clauses of the Karnataka Shops and Commercial Establishments Act through an amendment published in the Gazette on September 4, saying it removes safety and security measures for women on night shifts, including mandatory driver verification, authorised pick-up and drop routes, confidentiality of contact details, security guards, and emergency mechanisms. KITU demanded immediate withdrawal of amendments deleting Section 25(1)(h)–(o) and restoration of all statutory safeguards.

The Karnataka State IT/ITeS Employees' Union has condemned the omission of several clauses of the Karnataka Shops and Commercial Establishments Act through an amendment published in the Gazette on September 4, saying it removes safety and security measures for women on night shifts, including mandatory driver verification, authorised pick-up and drop routes, confidentiality of contact details, security guards, and emergency mechanisms. KITU demanded immediate withdrawal of amendments deleting Section 25(1)(h)–(o) and restoration of all statutory safeguards. Labour Department sources said the provisions are now covered under the Occupational Safety, Health and Working Conditions Code, as the Shops Act applies only to establishments employing fewer than 10 people while larger establishments fall under OSH.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** KITU flags deletion of women's night-shift safeguards; Labour Department says OSH Code covers them
- **Told:** The Karnataka State IT/ITeS Employees' Union has condemned the omission of several clauses of the Karnataka Shops and Commercial Establishments Act through an amendment published in the Gazette on September 4, saying it removes safety and security measures for women on night shifts, including mandatory driver verification, authorised pick-up and drop routes, confidentiality of contact details, security guards, and emergency mechanisms.
- **Purpose:** Labour Department sources said the provisions are now covered under the Occupational Safety, Health and Working Conditions Code, as the Shops Act applies only to establishments employing fewer than 10 people while larger establishments fall under OSH.

**Important points**
- Labour Department sources said the provisions are now covered under the Occupational Safety.
- Health and Working Conditions Code, as the Shops Act applies only to establishments employing fewer than 10 people while larger establishments fall under OSH.
_Sources: The Hindu Mangaluru p.5; Times of India Bengaluru p.5_

#### 'As long as I'm scoring runs, I'm happy'

**In short:** Karnataka batter Devdutt Padikkal scored two centuries in three innings on India's recent Test tour of Sri Lanka, amassing 328 runs and winning Player-of-the-Series honours after returning to the Test squad after nearly two years. The 26-year-old, who amassed 543 runs in six Ranji Trophy games last season, said domestic cricket makes a player tougher and that game time provides first-hand experience on how to model one's game, and he is looking forward to leading India-A against Australia-A later this month after captaining Karnataka from mid-season last year.

#### Union: Rollback of safety norms for women risky

**In short:** The Karnataka State IT/ITeS Employees Union has opposed the state government's decision to remove statutory safety and security measures for women working night shifts, saying the move could increase risks and dilute employer accountability. Deleted provisions had required safer late-night commutes through driver verification, route monitoring, protection of women's details, and rules on first pick-up and last drop-off.

#### 4-day crackdown: 800 children rescued from 17k workplaces

**In short:** Police rescued nearly 800 children working in hotels, garages, shops and other establishments after raids at nearly 17,000 premises across 962 police station limits in Karnataka following CM DK Shivakumar's directive, triggered after he reportedly saw a child labourer at Yeshwantpur APMC Yard on Sept 8. Bengaluru accounted for 503 rescues from 5,897 premises inspected under 188 police stations, with children paid Rs 10,000–Rs 16,000 monthly; FIRs were filed in five cases, 54 children handed to Child Welfare Committees, and rescued children included migrants from Bihar, Assam, West Bengal, Uttar Pradesh, Rajasthan, Jharkhand and Nepal.

#### Min: Will look into changing school timings

**In short:** School education and literacy minister Madhu Bangarappa said the state govt will look into changing school timings after MLC Puttanna raised the demand, citing Bengaluru's worsening traffic congestion affecting students' and teachers' daily commutes. Bangarappa said govt would consult various departments before deciding, and that the state was considering extending state-level teacher awards to deserving teachers in private schools.

#### Making A Difference: Workshops Shape Greener Ganesha Festival

**In short:** Ahead of Ganesh Chaturthi on Monday, citizen groups, lake activists and RWAs across Bengaluru are running free clay idol-making workshops to move families away from Plaster-of-Paris idols toward natural clay; groups such as Benniganahalli Kere Community Development Trust and HSR Citizen Forum have held such sessions for years. On Saturday actress Ragini Dwivedi joined a Bharatinagar Residents Forum demo in Cox Town, and city immersion arrangements include 659 mobile tanks across five corporations with single-window permit centres; organisers say making idols themselves helps children understand the environmental impact of festival choices.

#### Safe travels: RPF ups security for festive rush

**In short:** Following overcrowding at city railway stations from Friday evening, South Western Railway's Bengaluru division said additional Railway Protection Force personnel will be deployed at major stations, platforms, circulating areas and vulnerable locations during the Gauri Ganesha festival, with the RPF dog squad conducting anti-sabotage and explosive detection checks. In coordination with GRP and other agencies, RPF will maintain CCTV surveillance and crowd management; passengers are advised to remain vigilant, safeguard belongings and report suspicious activity to helpline 139 or nearest RPF/GRP personnel.

#### Opinions sought on bus fare revision by RTCs

**In short:** The Karnataka Public Transport Fare Regulatory Committee has invited suggestions and objections by Sept 21 on revising bus fares for KSRTC, BMTC, NWKRTC and KKRTC, citing rising diesel and fuel prices, higher staff salaries and operating costs; submissions can be sent to the committee chairperson at Shanti Nagar, Bengaluru, or via https://ksrtc.org/fare_revision_feedback/. The four corporations' combined liabilities stood at Rs 8,095.5 crore as of May 31, 2026, and they have also sought Rs 5,418.6 crore from govt as reimbursement under the Shakti scheme; bus fares were last increased by 15% in January 2025.

#### Anticipatory bail for Munirathna

**In short:** A special court for elected representatives has granted anticipatory bail till Sept 15 to RR Nagar MLA Munirathna Naidu in a criminal case registered for threatening a woman, who had lodged a complaint against the MLA and seven others accusing them of threatening her to withdraw a rape case she filed in 2021 against Ravi Shankar, a close associate of the MLA. The court said the petitioner shall be released if arrested upon executing a personal bond of Rs 2 lakh.

#### 2 rob woman of gold chain

**In short:** Two bike-borne miscreants snatched a 55g two-strand gold mangalya chain worth over Rs 7.5 lakh from Shivaranjini, 25, around 8am on Nayandahalli Metro Layout rail-underpass road in west Bengaluru as she walked to work at a fuel station; the pillion rider in a red jacket grabbed the chain after approaching from behind. She held on but suffered neck pain, was treated at a hospital and then approached police after passersby alerted them.

#### PLOGGING TO BOTANICAL ART, CITIZENS TURN UP FOR CITY

**In short:** The Times of India's #IamBengaluru – Volunteer for Parks campaign's fifth week began Saturday with a jog-and-plogging activity at Jakkur Park led by Jalaposhan Trust, a plogging and waste-segregation drive at RBI Layout Park No 2 by Youth for Seva that cumulatively collected 10 large bags of plastic waste, and a botanical art workshop at Koramangala 5th Block Park mentored by Pavithra S of Kiria Arts. National Public School Rajajinagar students demonstrated textile-waste segregation under Project Evara, and participants said the initiative helps reconnect Bengalureans with parks amid rapid digital-era change.

#### Rapido driver flees with rider's phone, seeks 18k

**In short:** Trideep Debnath, 25, filed a complaint at Madiwala police station after Rapido rider Ashutosh Kumar, who arrived on a white scooter instead of booked vehicle KA-01-HX-7232, fled with his Samsung Galaxy S24 Ultra worth around Rs 50,000 during a ride booked at 5.54am on Sept 6 from Narayanappa Garden 4th Cross to Marathahalli when Debnath stepped off at a Madiwala fuel station. The accused later contacted Debnep's sister via WhatsApp demanding Rs 18,000 to return the phone, threatening to destroy or sell it outside Karnataka, and sent a UPI payment scanner.

#### Family alleges social boycott over roadside structure

**In short:** A Scheduled Caste family from Kuruvangi village in Chikkamagaluru taluk has alleged social boycott after objecting to Venkatesh constructing a metal-sheet structure and installing an iron pole in a drain leading to welder KM Rajesh's house, obstructing movement; Rajesh told Chikkamagaluru Rural police Venkatesh attempted to assault him and his mother when questioned and that three high-voltage lines above pose a safety risk. Rajesh alleged Venkatesh, BJP SC Morcha district youth president and former ZP member, convened a meeting on Sept 6 around 7.30am to boycott the family, which a villager conveyed to Rajesh's brother Satish.

#### 'If only forests had Wi-Fi': Bittu Tabahi stunned by waste at Turahalli

**In short:** Madhya Pradesh environment activist Surendra Singh Choudhary, known as Bittu Tabahi, joined AN Yellappa Reddy and volunteers in a Clean Up Turahalli drive Saturday along the Banashankari 6th Stage periphery of Turahalli Reserve Forest, organised by The Indian Garage Co with Indus Herbs and GBA support, removing over two tonnes of legacy waste including poultry waste, sanitary waste, dog carcasses, medical waste and plastic over nearly four hours from 8am, requiring a backhoe. Bittu, a 21-year-old BSc student who spent Rs 30,000 cleaning three ghats of Ajnar river in Madhya Pradesh, said he expected Bengaluru's green spaces to be litter-free and remarked that if forests had Wi-Fi people might treat them with more regard.

**In short:** Kannada theatre director, scholar and arts administrator Chidambara Rao Jambe died in Shivamogga on Saturday; born on Jan 3, 1949, in Adderi village of Sagar taluk, he served as principal of the Ninasam Theatre Institute for over two decades.

#### Catch-22 for foresters as DKS warns against excessive action

**In short:** At the National Forest Martyrs' Day event at Aranya Bhavan on Friday, CM DK Shivakumar told forest officials the public do not accept them as readily as police officers and urged friendly relations with fringe communities, saying encroachments must be resolved legally without unnecessary pressure and that officials should not obstruct development projects or act excessively. A retired principal chief conservator of forests said the remarks could leave field staff caught between accusations of over-enforcement and being soft on encroachment, especially along forest boundaries where cultivation and construction expand into forest land.

#### Lovers found hanging on K'buragi hill

**In short:** Two construction labourers, Sharanamma Bhimanalli, 32, of Basava Nagar in Chittapur town, and Basavaraja Matter, 21, of Bachawara village in Yadgir district, were found hanging at Sankanur Hill in Chittapur taluk of Kalaburagi district on Saturday morning; police said the duo had been meeting frequently in Chittapur town over recent months while working as construction labourers. #### NOTIFICATION (Guru Teak Investments property attachment)

**In short:** Government of Karnataka Revenue Department order No. E-RD 17 GRC 2026 dated 29.08.2026 attaches immovable properties of Guru Teak Investments (Mysore) Private Limited and Guru Agri Farm Private Limited under the Karnataka Protection of Interest of Depositors in Financial Establishments Act, 2004, after investigations found clandestine public deposit collection and failure to return money with promised interest; properties believed acquired from those deposits vest with the Competent Authority from Official Gazette publication, and the annexure lists attached land in Malavalli taluk.

#### Vroom to volt

**In short:** Bengaluru buyers weighing an EV this festive season should look beyond design and fuel savings, experts say. Sunil Pai, MD and CEO of a multi-brand automotive retail group, cited FAME-scheme momentum, 500 km-plus ranges, fast-charging on highways, and EVs’ tech and comfort edge; electric two-wheelers lead local adoption for affordability and city use.

Bengaluru buyers weighing an EV this festive season should look beyond design and fuel savings, experts say. Sunil Pai, MD and CEO of a multi-brand automotive retail group, cited FAME-scheme momentum, 500 km-plus ranges, fast-charging on highways, and EVs’ tech and comfort edge; electric two-wheelers lead local adoption for affordability and city use. Abin Varghese, director of an electric mobility firm, advised matching real-world range to your commute, securing home or workplace charging—overnight charging works best—and evaluating battery tech, warranty, OTA updates, service network, spare parts and roadside assistance rather than buying solely to cut fuel costs; a test drive should assess torque, regenerative braking, modes and ergonomics.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Vroom to volt
- **Told:** Bengaluru buyers weighing an EV this festive season should look beyond design and fuel savings, experts say.
- **Purpose:** Abin Varghese, director of an electric mobility firm, advised matching real-world range to your commute, securing home or workplace charging—overnight charging works best—and evaluating battery tech, warranty, OTA updates, service network, spare parts and roadside assistance rather than buying solely to cut fuel costs; a test drive should assess torque, regenerative braking, modes and ergonomics.

**Important points**
- Abin Varghese, director of an electric mobility firm, advised matching real-world range to your commute, securing home or workplace charging—overnight charging works best—and evaluating battery tech, warranty.
- OTA updates, service network, spare parts and roadside assistance rather than buying solely to cut fuel costs.
- a test drive should assess torque, regenerative braking, modes and ergonomics.
_Sources: Times of India Bengaluru p.25_

### India

#### BRICS condemns Pahalgam terror attack in unison

**In short:** The BRICS New Delhi Declaration adopted Saturday strongly condemned the April 2025 Pahalgam terror attack that killed 26 people, mainly tourists, called for zero tolerance toward cross-border terrorism and accountability for perpetrators and sponsors, urged expeditious finalisation of the UN Comprehensive Convention on International Terrorism, and welcomed BRICS Counter-Terrorism Working Group activities; BJP spokesperson Pradeep Bhandari called it a significant diplomatic win for India.

The BRICS New Delhi Declaration adopted Saturday strongly condemned the April 2025 Pahalgam terror attack that killed 26 people, mainly tourists, called for zero tolerance toward cross-border terrorism and accountability for perpetrators and sponsors, urged expeditious finalisation of the UN Comprehensive Convention on International Terrorism, and welcomed BRICS Counter-Terrorism Working Group activities; BJP spokesperson Pradeep Bhandari called it a significant diplomatic win for India.

#### Xi and Modi welcome 'steady progress' in ties

**In short:** India and China expressed commitment to a fair, reasonable and mutually acceptable resolution of the boundary question, proceeding from the political perspective of overall bilateral relations and the long-term interests of the two peoples, the External Affairs Ministry said after the leaders' meeting. A Chinese Ministry of Foreign Affairs statement said both sides should pursue progress on bilateral relations and the boundary question in parallel, ensuring each supports the other and maintaining peace and tranquillity in border areas.

India and China expressed commitment to a fair, reasonable and mutually acceptable resolution of the boundary question, proceeding from the political perspective of overall bilateral relations and the long-term interests of the two peoples, the External Affairs Ministry said after the leaders' meeting. A Chinese Ministry of Foreign Affairs statement said both sides should pursue progress on bilateral relations and the boundary question in parallel, ensuring each supports the other and maintaining peace and tranquillity in border areas. Mr. Xi arrived with a large delegation for a two-day visit and was driven to Bharat Mandapam amid tight security; the two leaders agreed to continue expanding common ground in resolving regional and global issues while addressing challenges. Speaking at the summit on West Asia, Mr. Xi said the war has inflicted serious losses on people across the region and Chinese Foreign Ministry spokesperson Mao Ning called for a political settlement, with Mr. Xi saying China would work with fellow BRICS members to bring peace and tranquillity to the Middle East and that India and China, as key Global South members, bear significant responsibilities in world affairs.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Xi and Modi welcome 'steady progress' in ties
- **Told:** India and China expressed commitment to a fair, reasonable and mutually acceptable resolution of the boundary question, proceeding from the political perspective of overall bilateral relations and the long-term interests of the two peoples, the External Affairs Ministry said after the leaders' meeting.
- **Purpose:** Xi saying China would work with fellow BRICS members to bring peace and tranquillity to the Middle East and that India and China, as key Global South members, bear significant responsibilities in world affairs.

**Important points**
- Mr.
- Xi arrived with a large delegation for a two-day visit and was driven to Bharat Mandapam amid tight security.
- the two leaders agreed to continue expanding common ground in resolving regional and global issues while addressing challenges.
- Speaking at the summit on West Asia, Mr.

#### HC: ‘UP using Goonda Act as tool of oppression’

**In short:** The Allahabad High Court Lucknow bench said Uttar Pradesh seems to be using the Goondas Act as a tool of oppression while hearing Zahid Ali’s challenge to being declared a goonda and externed from Gonda district for six months, days after it quashed Delhi University student Akriti Chaudhary’s NSA detention over Noida worker protests in April.

#### NIA court issues arrest warrant against LeT chief

**In short:** A special National Investigation Agency court in Jammu issued a non-bailable arrest warrant against Lashkar-e-Taiba founder Hafiz Muhammad Saeed in a case related to providing weapons, money and directions to terrorists operating in the Kashmir Valley, after the NIA moved under Section 75 of the Bharatiya Nagarik Suraksha Sanhita alleging he operates from Pakistan and is deliberately evading arrest. The case stems from the arrest this year of Mohammad Naqeeb Bhat in Ganderbal with a Chinese grenade and 15 live rounds, linked to LeT/The Resistance Front; the agency also searched 10 locations in Kupwara, Kulgam, Bandipora, Baramulla and Sopore in the Zakoora case and said it found linkages between Pakistan-based LeT handlers and local associates.

#### Farmers’ alliance seeks statute on seed rights

**In short:** Bharat Beej Swaraj Manch urged legislation ensuring farmers’ natural seed rights are not subordinate to breeders’ rights under the PPV&FR Act, a month after the Supreme Court in Kavita Kuruganti vs PepsiCo India agreed with the Delhi High Court that farmers claiming protection under the Act may do so while PepsiCo retains legal remedies if its registered potato variety rights are infringed.

#### Maharashtra: ST discontent wells up during Ganeshotsav

**In short:** Scheduled Tribe grievances over Forest Rights Act implementation, hostel conditions, PESA appointments and Dhangar ST inclusion demands persist after the 2018 Nashik-Mumbai farmers’ march, with recent snakebite deaths of three tribal ashram hostel girls in Gadchiroli and a fever death in Palghar intensifying protests Rahul Gandhi amplified; the Fadnavis government formed a high-level recruitment review committee after Pune and Nashik agitations, a 17-day Manjari hostel fast ending after Tribal Development Minister Ashok Uike’s assurances, and Sakal Adivasi Vidyarthi Andolan Kruti Samiti planning a September 15–20 Nashik-Mumbai march during Ganeshotsav.

#### Tata Sons can't surrender NBFC licence: RBI

**In short:** The Reserve Bank of India has rejected Tata Sons' application to surrender its Non-Banking Finance Company licence, saying the application "cannot be acceded to" and asking the Tata Group holding company to prepare for an immediate public listing, putting an end to years of uncertainty. Tata Sons had applied for de-registration as it was majority-owned by charitable trusts and was not mobilising public funds, but the RBI included it in the list of 17 large upper-layer NBFCs for 2026-27 subject to enhanced regulatory requirements and mandatory listing within three years of identification.

The Reserve Bank of India has rejected Tata Sons' application to surrender its Non-Banking Finance Company licence, saying the application "cannot be acceded to" and asking the Tata Group holding company to prepare for an immediate public listing, putting an end to years of uncertainty. Tata Sons had applied for de-registration as it was majority-owned by charitable trusts and was not mobilising public funds, but the RBI included it in the list of 17 large upper-layer NBFCs for 2026-27 subject to enhanced regulatory requirements and mandatory listing within three years of identification. With listing imminent, Tata Sons will face enhanced governance and disclosures amid allegations of governance lapses and a leadership transition yet to be finalised by Tata Trusts.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Tata Sons can't surrender NBFC licence: RBI
- **Told:** The Reserve Bank of India has rejected Tata Sons' application to surrender its Non-Banking Finance Company licence, saying the application "cannot be acceded to" and asking the Tata Group holding company to prepare for an immediate public listing, putting an end to years of uncertainty.

#### EC meets both Trinamool Congress factions, seeks more documents from Ritabrata camp

**In short:** The Election Commission met both Trinamool Congress factions — Mamata Banerjee-led and Ritabrata Banerjee-led — over claims to the party name and 'joda ghash phool' symbol ahead of October 6 Assembly bypolls in Nandigram and Rejinagar, for which nominations opened Wednesday and close on September 16. Ritabrata claims support of over 65 Trinamool MLAs of 80 in the 294-member Assembly; the EC sought additional documents from his faction and, citing Shiv Sena precedent, sources said the symbol may be frozen temporarily, while Mamata's delegation insisted there was no dispute and said defectors who received ₹25 lakh each to contest elections five months ago were traitors.

#### Amid ED action on aide, Rawat offers to quit two Cong. posts

**In short:** Former Uttarakhand Chief Minister Harish Rawat offered to step down as executive member of the Uttarakhand Pradesh Congress Committee and as permanent invitee to the Congress Working Committee after the Enforcement Directorate searched the house of his former officer on special duty Chandan Singh Jeena over alleged irregularities in recruitment examinations during Rawat's tenure. Rawat said no weakness should enter the party's anti-corruption struggle on his account and called the ED action a narrative-building attempt; Uttarakhand Congress president Ganesh Godiyal said the 2016 examination was cancelled and investigated under Rawat's government and questioned why the Centre acted after 11 years.

#### Hyderabad man loses Rs 3.35 cr to investment scam

**In short:** Telangana Cyber Security Bureau registered a case after a man reportedly lost Rs 3.35 crore over about 55 days to a fraudster who contacted him via a dating app, built trust on WhatsApp, and lured him into a fake Meta-related investment app showing apparent profits before demanding more money when he tried to withdraw, citing a lowered credit score.

**In short:** JD(U) working president Sanjay Jha proposed renaming Janata Dal (United) as Janata Dal (Nitish), while HAM president Santosh Suman suggested renaming Nitish Kumar’s birthplace Bakhtiyarpur as ‘Nitishpur’; senior minister Shrawan Kumar and LJP(R) president Chirag Paswan backed the tributes, though Jan Suraaj Party founder Prashant Kishor said the party is controlled by Amit Shah appointees serving BJP interests.

#### US finalises levies on solar cell imports from India

**In short:** The US Department of Commerce on Friday finalised anti-dumping margins of 123.04% for Indian producers, 94.36% for Indonesia and 65.43% for Laos, and countervailing duty rates of 126.09% for India, 73.2–173.7% for Indonesia and 82.03–153.67% for Laos on crystalline silicon photovoltaic cells, following complaints of unfair subsidies harming American solar manufacturing; the US International Trade Commission will decide by October 14 whether to issue duty orders by November 2 or terminate the probe.

The US Department of Commerce on Friday finalised anti-dumping margins of 123.04% for Indian producers, 94.36% for Indonesia and 65.43% for Laos, and countervailing duty rates of 126.09% for India, 73.2–173.7% for Indonesia and 82.03–153.67% for Laos on crystalline silicon photovoltaic cells, following complaints of unfair subsidies harming American solar manufacturing; the US International Trade Commission will decide by October 14 whether to issue duty orders by November 2 or terminate the probe.

**What this is**

#### India eye record eighth title

**In short:** Seven-time champions India will look to Shafali Verma (168 runs, two fifties, tournament’s leading scorer) and Deepti Sharma (11 wickets, joint top with Chamari Athapaththu) when they face Sri Lanka in the Women’s T20 Asia Cup final in Dubai on Sunday. Harmanpreet Kaur’s side remain wary after Sri Lanka’s eight-wicket win in the 2024 final and their semifinal recovery from 5/2 and 33/4 to chase 131 against Pakistan, while India’s fielding concerns persist with nine dropped catches in four matches.

#### India brace for tricky Afg test

**In short:** India face a selection dilemma between Vaibhav Sooryavanshi’s aggression and Sanju Samson’s experience in the first T20I against Afghanistan in New Delhi on Sunday, with both possibly playing if Ishan Kishan is ruled out after a back scare. Shreyas Iyer’s side, preparing for the Asian Games, will test themselves against Rashid Khan, Noor Ahmed and Mujeeb ur Rahman in a three-match series where India lead the bilateral T20I head-to-head 8-0 with one no-result in nine meetings.

#### Amount of cricket the main reason for injuries: Iyer

**In short:** India T20I captain Shreyas Iyer said he avoids burdening players with rigid roles, arguing that continuous high-intensity cricket makes injuries inevitable rather than deliberate, and that backups must be ready when combinations change. Ahead of the Afghanistan T20I, he welcomed Jasprit Bumrah’s return from injury and called having Sooryavanshi, Samson and Abhishek Sharma in the squad a luxury for the opening slot.

#### Man murders wife and 3 daughters, serves tea to father

**In short:** Rahul Jha allegedly killed his wife Geeta and daughters Nandini, 23, Radhika, 18, and a 14-year-old at their Jaipur Kardhani home, then served tea to his elderly father the next morning before fleeing. Police recovered the stone used in the attack; Jha, a share trader who had borrowed Rs 25,000 days earlier, is being sought as financial distress and family disputes are probed.

#### GDP BOOM, PEOPLE'S GLOOM

**In short:** India's GDP grew 7.8% in April-June 2026, adding nearly Rs 5.89 lakh crore, while the RBI's FCNR(B) scheme drew a record $136 billion (about Rs 12 lakh crore), but sugar has jumped 30-40% to Rs 61-68 a kg across cities and milk, vegetables and eggs are 15-35% costlier. Muralidhar Swaminathan writes that headline growth and forex inflows have not eased the common citizen's cost-of-living squeeze.

#### Modi, Xi 'express commitment' to resolving border issue

**In short:** PM Narendra Modi and Chinese President Xi Jinping agreed to work towards a fair, reasonable and mutually acceptable boundary resolution during their meeting on the BRICS summit sidelines in New Delhi, India's foreign ministry said. Modi underlined that both sides should observe existing border agreements; the meeting is Xi's first visit to India since 2019 amid a cautious thaw after the 2020 Galwan clash.

#### Modi urges BRICS leaders to pitch in for global reform

**In short:** Highlighting the imbalance between decision-makers and those impacted by their decisions, Prime Minister Narendra Modi on Saturday made a strong pitch for global reform and called for BRICS to transition from "rule-takers to rule-shapers". In his welcome address at the BRICS leaders' gathering in New Delhi, he proposed that the leaders arrive at 10 proposals for global governance reform that can be developed into a BRICS Reform Roadmap.

**In short:** The Hindu deeply regrets the publication in its Delhi, Mohali, and North India editions of an offensive front-page advertisement titled "What is Dera Sacha Sauda? (Facts vs Fiction)" alleging, among other things, that there was a conspiracy against the convicted rapist, Gurmeet Ram Rahim Singh, head of the Dera Sacha Sauda, by "drug mafias and forces opposed to the Dera."
