#### A.P. MedTech Zone to drive BRICS BioDesign initiative

**In short:** The Andhra Pradesh MedTech Zone (AMTZ) is spearheading the BRICS BioDesign Programme to advance cross-border healthcare innovation and medical technology across member nations. Principal Scientific Adviser Ajay Kumar Sood launched the initiative in New Delhi on Friday, with participating institutions exchanging a Memorandum of Agreement; it operates through AMTZ, the Office of the Principal Scientific Adviser, FICCI, and the Science & Technology Cluster.

The Andhra Pradesh MedTech Zone (AMTZ) is spearheading the BRICS BioDesign Programme to advance cross-border healthcare innovation and medical technology across member nations. Principal Scientific Adviser Ajay Kumar Sood launched the initiative in New Delhi on Friday, with participating institutions exchanging a Memorandum of Agreement; it operates through AMTZ, the Office of the Principal Scientific Adviser, FICCI, and the Science & Technology Cluster. AMTZ Managing Director Jitendra Sharma said it builds pathways from science to patients, enabling co-development, investment, and accessible medical technologies across emerging economies.

#### Arms theft case: police to seek custody of ex-Havildar

**In short:** Malkajgiri police will file a petition before the court on Tuesday seeking seven days' custody of former Army Havaldar Upputholla Mallesh, 37, arrested in connection with the theft of firearms and ammunition from the 20 Battalion, Madras Regiment, in Bolarum. Mallesh, who retired from the Army on June 30 after 16 years of service, was arrested on Friday after investigators recovered all the stolen firearms and ammunition from different locations.

#### ED's charges reflect shared agenda of Cong., BJP: Pinarayi

**In short:** Leader of the Opposition Pinarayi Vijayan said there was something politically uncanny about the Enforcement Directorate seeking the cooperation of the Congress-ruled Keralam government to implicate him in the CMRL-Exalogic payoff case. At a press conference in Kannur on Saturday, he accused the ED of resurrecting false charges already thrown out by the Vigilance Court, Kerala High Court and Supreme Court, and wondered whether the move was a shared political agenda of the Union and State governments.

Leader of the Opposition Pinarayi Vijayan said there was something politically uncanny about the Enforcement Directorate seeking the cooperation of the Congress-ruled Keralam government to implicate him in the CMRL-Exalogic payoff case. #### 1.4 lakh MBBS seats available in 836 colleges after revision

**In short:** The National Medical Commission has revised the MBBS seat matrix for the 2026-27 academic year, cautioning medical colleges and counselling authorities against admitting students beyond regulator-approved intake. The updated matrix lists 1,40,214 MBBS seats across 836 medical colleges — 63,971 in 391 government colleges and 76,243 in 445 private colleges — incorporating permissions for new colleges and increased intake at existing ones; the NMC said the matrix is not necessarily final.

#### Fugitive gangster extradited from Moldova: Punjab Police

**In short:** Punjab Police said fugitive criminal Amritpal Singh, alias Amrit Dalam, a key operative of the Jaggu Bhagwanpuria gang, was extradited from the Republic of Moldova with assistance from Central agencies. Wanted in multiple cases of murder, attempt to murder, UAPA, Arms Act and narcotics offences, his return marks the first extradition secured by Punjab Police from a European country, Director-General of Police Gaurav Yadav said, calling it a breakthrough against transnational organised crime.

#### Air India pilot found dead in hotel; police rule out foul play

**In short:** An Air India pilot in Delhi for Boeing 787 conversion training died after being found unconscious in his hotel room in Vasant Kunj during a layover; he had checked in on Thursday and was found dead the next evening. The pilot, who had worked with Air India Express, was undergoing conversion training for wide-body Boeing 787 aircraft; a post-mortem was conducted at Safdarjung Hospital and the final cause of death awaits the medical opinion, while police said they were not suspecting foul play.

#### Body of Jaish terrorist found in Doda; M4 rifle recovered

**In short:** Jammu and Kashmir Police recovered the body of a Pakistani Jaish-e-Mohammed terrorist from the upper reaches of Chenab Valley's Doda district on Saturday. The police Special Operations Group, Army's Rashtriya Rifles and the CRPF retrieved remains of a terrorist neutralised earlier in a joint operation, and a night-enabled M4 rifle was also recovered; police have stepped up area domination and patrolling in response to a change in terrorist tactics.

#### Ladakh talks fruitful, but Wangchuk, others misled people: ex-MP

**In short:** Former BJP MP from Ladakh Thupstan Chhewang said September 9 talks between Ladakhi civil society leaders, the Union Ministry of Home Affairs and the UT administration were "fruitful", countering the disappointment expressed by Leh Apex Body, Kargil Democratic Alliance and activist Sonam Wangchuk. Ladakh Chief Secretary Ashish Kundra had proposed a sui generis model under Article 371 with legislative, executive and financial powers, but LAB and KDA called the meeting a waste of time; Chhewang said positive consensus points were not brought before the public and criticised Wangchuk's reported call for renewed agitation with an ultimatum.

#### Delhi slum covered with large sheets amid summit

**In short:** With the BRICS summit under way in Delhi, Coolie camp, a slum settlement in south Delhi's Vasant Vihar housing about 281 households per 2012 DUSIB data, was covered with large white and purple sheets on the night of September 10 to hide it from foreign dignitaries, residents said. Ten-foot curtains along Nelson Mandela Marg and Munirka Marg restrict entry and exit to two points; residents said a similar cover-up with green curtains occurred during the 2023 G-20 Summit and complained that sanitation workers living there were being hidden rather than helped.

#### India must give BRICS moral leadership and strategic clarity: Cong.

**In short:** The Congress urged the Indian government to take moral leadership of BRICS and use its chairmanship to restore the diplomatic stature India historically commanded as a voice of the Global South. #### How Delhi is managing air traffic during the summit

**In short:** Behind the ceremonial welcomes for BRICS leaders lies a tightly coordinated aviation operation involving around 35 special aircraft, multiple airports, controlled airspace, and carefully timed movements to accommodate foreign dignitaries without disrupting Delhi's regular commercial flights. The operation spans Indira Gandhi International Airport, the Indian Air Force's Palam technical area and other parking airports, with the CISF, IAF, aviation authorities and other security agencies coordinating movements while VVIP and regular passenger flows are kept separate.

The operation spans Indira Gandhi International Airport, the Indian Air Force's Palam technical area and other parking airports, with the CISF, IAF, aviation authorities and other security agencies coordinating movements while VVIP and regular passenger flows are kept separate. The Directorate General of Civil Aviation has also imposed restrictions on non-scheduled flight operations.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** How Delhi is managing air traffic during the summit

#### Zomato starts levying additional fee on COD orders

**In short:** Online food delivery platform Zomato has introduced a separate fee on cash-on-delivery orders, with the charges varying across users and order values, checks on the company's app showed on Saturday. The new charge, shown as a "Pay on Delivery Fee" in the order bill, applies when customers choose to pay in cash at delivery, with different amounts appearing for different transaction values; the exact basis for determining the fee could not be immediately ascertained and an email seeking comments from Zomato went unanswered.

#### SEBI proposes changes to CAS format after industry feedback

**In short:** The Securities and Exchange Board of India has proposed to review expiry-day settlement prices, market timings, order handling and information dissemination under the newly introduced Closing Auction Session, following industry feedback on initial operational snags. In a consultation paper released on September 12, SEBI said the existing methodology for determining expiry-day settlement prices for index and stock derivatives needs review amid heightened activity in expiring index options and IEP-based derivatives trading during CAS.

The Securities and Exchange Board of India has proposed to review expiry-day settlement prices, market timings, order handling and information dissemination under the newly introduced Closing Auction Session, following industry feedback on initial operational snags. In a consultation paper released on September 12, SEBI said the existing methodology for determining expiry-day settlement prices for index and stock derivatives needs review amid heightened activity in expiring index options and IEP-based derivatives trading during CAS. SEBI proposed two alternatives using a blended volume-weighted average price or temporarily reverting to the pre-CAS settlement-price mechanism, and proposed discontinuing the display of indicative index value while continuing to provide the IEP for individual securities.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** SEBI proposes changes to CAS format after industry feedback
- **Told:** The Securities and Exchange Board of India has proposed to review expiry-day settlement prices, market timings, order handling and information dissemination under the newly introduced Closing Auction Session, following industry feedback on initial operational snags.
- **Purpose:** SEBI proposed two alternatives using a blended volume-weighted average price or temporarily reverting to the pre-CAS settlement-price mechanism, and proposed discontinuing the display of indicative index value while continuing to provide the IEP for individual securities.

**Important points**
- SEBI proposed two alternatives using a blended volume-weighted average price or temporarily reverting to the pre-CAS settlement-price mechanism, and proposed discontinuing the display of indicative index value while continuing to provide the IEP for individual securities.
_Sources: The Hindu Mangaluru p.9_

U.S.-seized tankers Marinera and Lileo have been brought to Alang in Gujarat for recycling by GMS, joining a growing flow of sanctioned vessels to India as Bangladesh and Pakistan refuse such ships and blockades and interdiction in the seas make operating them more challenging. Indian banks and financial firms will not accept transactions involving U.S.-sanctioned entities, but industry sources say Indian firms in the chain conduct immediate transactions in rupees while money may be routed through other channels; the U.S. may approve demolitions on a case-by-case basis if proceeds do not fund activities it opposes. #### Senior living market can grow into a $10.1 bn prospect: ASLI-JLL

**In short:** India's senior living market is expected to become a $10.1 billion opportunity by 2030, requiring $7.7 billion capital investment to meet new supply under a policy-driven scenario, according to an ASLI-JLL report titled "India's Silver Economy: From Niche to Necessity". With over 166 million Indians aged above 60 projected to double by 2050, the organised senior living sector reached 25,050 dwelling units as of June 2026 at 1.5% penetration compared with 6-7% in the U.S.

India's senior living market is expected to become a $10.1 billion opportunity by 2030, requiring $7.7 billion capital investment to meet new supply under a policy-driven scenario, according to an ASLI-JLL report titled "India's Silver Economy: From Niche to Necessity". With over 166 million Indians aged above 60 projected to double by 2050, the organised senior living sector reached 25,050 dwelling units as of June 2026 at 1.5% penetration compared with 6-7% in the U.S. and 14-15% in New Zealand, registering 14.2% CAGR between 2024 and the first half of 2026. The addressable market of urban, financially independent senior households is expected to grow from 1.7 million in 2026 to 2.1 million by 2030, spanning assisted care, healthcare, rehabilitation and supporting services.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Senior living market can grow into a $10.1 bn prospect: ASLI-JLL
- **Told:** India's senior living market is expected to become a $10.1 billion opportunity by 2030, requiring $7.7 billion capital investment to meet new supply under a policy-driven scenario, according to an ASLI-JLL report titled "India's Silver Economy: From Niche to Necessity".
- **Purpose:** The addressable market of urban, financially independent senior households is expected to grow from 1.7 million in 2026 to 2.1 million by 2030, spanning assisted care, healthcare, rehabilitation and supporting services.

**Important points**
- and 14-15% in New Zealand, registering 14.2% CAGR between 2024 and the first half of 2026.
- The addressable market of urban, financially independent senior households is expected to grow from 1.7 million in 2026 to 2.1 million by 2030, spanning assisted care, healthcare, rehabilitation and supporting services.

#### Rapid Himalayan erosion triggers hidden source of CO2: study

**In short:** A study by researchers from IISER Pune, the Wadia Institute of Himalayan Geology, and IIT-Roorkee published in Chemical Geology suggests that in glacier-fed river catchments of the upper Indus basin, CO2 release from pyrite oxidation may be three times higher than CO2 uptake by silicate weathering, with steep mountainous regions acting as net CO2 sources while flatter floodplains downstream remain sinks. As Himalayan glaciers crush rocks and expose pyrites to air and water, sulphides oxidise to sulphuric acid that reacts with carbonate rocks to release CO2, meaning glacier-driven erosion may influence long-term carbon cycles and contribute to climate warming over million-year timescales even as silicate weathering has helped cool the earth over the last 50 million years.

#### Study finds far fewer genes truly linked to cerebral palsy

**In short:** A new study in The American Journal of Human Genetics found that of 515 candidate genes reported across 21 published genomic diagnostic studies involving 5,440 individuals diagnosed with cerebral palsy, rigorous statistical analysis could confirm a true association for only 89, and in a cohort of 460 patients sequenced across the Shriners Children's hospital network, stricter proof whittled 60 disease-causing mutations down to 16 genes. In India, where three in every 1,000 live births are affected by cerebral palsy and genetic testing is a rapidly growing field, acting on false alarms from standard panels can divert resources from effective treatments, while distinguishing true genetic mimics from coincidental mutations can connect families to precise care faster.

#### Why are bank employees striking despite the PLI pause?

**In short:** The United Forum of Bank Unions called a nationwide strike on September 11 to protest a revised performance-linked incentive scheme announced in November 2024 that applies only to Scale IV officers and above, allowing up to 365 days of basic pay based on individual performance rather than the uniform 15 days basic pay plus dearness allowance formula for the rest of the workforce and departing from a 2020 agreement linking PLI to overall bank performance. Despite the government putting the PLI on hold after a September 8 meeting with a delegation led by the Bharatiya Mazdoor Sangh, unions proceeded with the strike because there was no progress on their demand for a five-day work week and other unresolved issues including uniform dearness allowance for pensioners and an NPS-to-OPS switch option.

The United Forum of Bank Unions called a nationwide strike on September 11 to protest a revised performance-linked incentive scheme announced in November 2024 that applies only to Scale IV officers and above, allowing up to 365 days of basic pay based on individual performance rather than the uniform 15 days basic pay plus dearness allowance formula for the rest of the workforce and departing from a 2020 agreement linking PLI to overall bank performance. Despite the government putting the PLI on hold after a September 8 meeting with a delegation led by the Bharatiya Mazdoor Sangh, unions proceeded with the strike because there was no progress on their demand for a five-day work week and other unresolved issues including uniform dearness allowance for pensioners and an NPS-to-OPS switch option. The strike disrupted branch banking for four days across many regions as it fell before the second Saturday, Sunday and Ganesh Chaturthi holidays, and unions have threatened a three-day strike from September 28 to 30 and a continuous indefinite strike from October 26 if demands remain unaddressed.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Why are bank employees striking despite the PLI pause?
- **Told:** The United Forum of Bank Unions called a nationwide strike on September 11 to protest a revised performance-linked incentive scheme announced in November 2024 that applies only to Scale IV officers and above, allowing up to 365 days of basic pay based on individual performance rather than the uniform 15 days basic pay plus dearness allowance formula for the rest of the workforce and departing from a 2020 agreement linking PLI to overall bank performance.
- **Purpose:** The strike disrupted branch banking for four days across many regions as it fell before the second Saturday, Sunday and Ganesh Chaturthi holidays, and unions have threatened a three-day strike from September 28 to 30 and a continuous indefinite strike from October 26 if demands remain unaddressed.

**Important points**
- The strike disrupted branch banking for four days across many regions as it fell before the second Saturday.
- Sunday and Ganesh Chaturthi holidays, and unions have threatened a three-day strike from September 28 to 30 and a continuous indefinite strike from October 26 if demands remain unaddressed.
_Sources: The Hindu Mangaluru p.11_

#### Why has the Census been deferred in Manipur?

**In short:** The Centre deferred Census operations in Manipur on August 31 amid demands from several community-based and civil society organisations to first conduct the State's National Register of Citizens exercise with 1951 as the base year, following Union Home Minister Amit Shah's meeting with the Governor and Chief Minister as the State heads toward Assembly elections less than a year away. Meiteis, Meitei Pangals and Nagas opposed the Census while the Kuki-Zo Council supported it, fearing respectively that an NRC with a 1951 base year could unfairly exclude Kukis or that Census without NRC would skew delimitation and representation in the ethnically divided State where conflict since May 2023 has claimed almost 300 lives and displaced more than 62,000 people.

The Centre deferred Census operations in Manipur on August 31 amid demands from several community-based and civil society organisations to first conduct the State's National Register of Citizens exercise with 1951 as the base year, following Union Home Minister Amit Shah's meeting with the Governor and Chief Minister as the State heads toward Assembly elections less than a year away. Meiteis, Meitei Pangals and Nagas opposed the Census while the Kuki-Zo Council supported it, fearing respectively that an NRC with a 1951 base year could unfairly exclude Kukis or that Census without NRC would skew delimitation and representation in the ethnically divided State where conflict since May 2023 has claimed almost 300 lives and displaced more than 62,000 people. Pro-NRC groups claim an NRC is necessary to identify "illegal migrants" from Bangladesh, Myanmar and Mizoram, though Assam's updated NRC process remains incomplete and contested, with the Registrar General of India yet to declare the 2019 draft as final.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Why has the Census been deferred in Manipur?
- **Told:** The Centre deferred Census operations in Manipur on August 31 amid demands from several community-based and civil society organisations to first conduct the State's National Register of Citizens exercise with 1951 as the base year, following Union Home Minister Amit Shah's meeting with the Governor and Chief Minister as the State heads toward Assembly elections less than a year away.
- **Purpose:** Pro-NRC groups claim an NRC is necessary to identify "illegal migrants" from Bangladesh, Myanmar and Mizoram, though Assam's updated NRC process remains incomplete and contested, with the Registrar General of India yet to declare the 2019 draft as final.

**Important points**
- Pro-NRC groups claim an NRC is necessary to identify "illegal migrants" from Bangladesh.
- Myanmar and Mizoram, though Assam's updated NRC process remains incomplete and contested, with the Registrar General of India yet to declare the 2019 draft as final.

#### Bin Laden considered targeting India and other countries in 1998: CIA papers

**In short:** India features among countries that former al-Qaeda chief Osama bin Laden had at one point considered targeting, according to CIA intelligence briefs released on Friday to mark the 25th anniversary of the September 11, 2001 attacks. An August 28, 1998 memo says Bin Laden survived a U.S.

India features among countries that former al-Qaeda chief Osama bin Laden had at one point considered targeting, according to CIA intelligence briefs released on Friday to mark the 25th anniversary of the September 11, 2001 attacks. An August 28, 1998 memo says Bin Laden survived a U.S. strike in Afghanistan and was intending to conduct attacks in Egypt, the Arabian Peninsula, India, Pakistan, Uganda and possibly Nigeria, while a September 1998 brief says his preferred target was U.S. soil and included a potential plan to fly an explosive-packed plane into a U.S. city and noted coordination with Harakat ul-Mujahedin to attack U.S., British and French interests.

#### India-China flight services to resume after a gap of 6 years

**In short:** China Southern Airlines announced it will resume regular passenger services on the Guangzhou-New Delhi route from September 21, restarting the service after a six-year suspension and operating more than 100 weekly flights across eight round-trip routes in South Asia. The announcement coincides with Chinese President Xi Jinping's visit to India; flight services between the two countries were suspended following the COVID-19 pandemic in 2020 and were not restored amid the over four-year military stand-off in eastern Ladakh, which ended in October last year.

#### A village with no people

**In short:** In just 10 years, Gattumeedapalle in Annamayya district of Andhra Pradesh has emptied out into a ghost village after repeated crop failures and depleting groundwater forced nearly 300 acres of groundnut cultivation and the community itself to wither away. Located about 125 km from Tirupati, the village that once sprawled across nearly 1,000 acres saw families gradually move to Kosuvaripalle or nearby Madanapalle from 2015 onward, leaving behind locked houses, abandoned furniture and electronic items that were looted including a temple bell, while former farmer Thimmakkagari Subbi Reddy, now a shepherd, says he has written off the village permanently.

In just 10 years, Gattumeedapalle in Annamayya district of Andhra Pradesh has emptied out into a ghost village after repeated crop failures and depleting groundwater forced nearly 300 acres of groundnut cultivation and the community itself to wither away. Located about 125 km from Tirupati, the village that once sprawled across nearly 1,000 acres saw families gradually move to Kosuvaripalle or nearby Madanapalle from 2015 onward, leaving behind locked houses, abandoned furniture and electronic items that were looted including a temple bell, while former farmer Thimmakkagari Subbi Reddy, now a shepherd, says he has written off the village permanently. Only a large mobile phone tower now watches from atop the hillock where activity once bustled.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** A village with no people
- **Told:** In just 10 years, Gattumeedapalle in Annamayya district of Andhra Pradesh has emptied out into a ghost village after repeated crop failures and depleting groundwater forced nearly 300 acres of groundnut cultivation and the community itself to wither away.
- **Purpose:** Only a large mobile phone tower now watches from atop the hillock where activity once bustled.

**Important points**
- Only a large mobile phone tower now watches from atop the hillock where activity once bustled.
_Sources: The Hindu Mangaluru p.16_

#### PIO missing in Nepal; help denied as he holds Indian passport: UK MP

**In short:** Dr Anirban Bhattacharyya, 66, a Newcastle University research associate who has lived in Britain for more than 60 years, remains on Nepal Police's missing-persons list over two weeks after Nepal's Aug 26 floods, with his constituency MP Ian Lavery alleging authorities declined to assist because he holds an Indian rather than British passport. Bhattacharyya was travelling with Kolkata-based Chalo Jaai Travel Club on a Kailash Mansarovar pilgrimage handled in Nepal by Samrat Tours and Travels and is among 32 contingent members still missing near Timure-Rasuwagadhi.

#### Brics bridges Iran-UAE gulf, clears way for jt declaration

**In short:** Brics members agreed on a 140-paragraph New Delhi Declaration after intensive negotiations until 4am Saturday, overcoming sharp UAE-Iran differences over the West Asia conflict through a rare leader-level meeting between Iran President Masoud Pezeshkian and Abu Dhabi Crown Prince Sheikh Khaled bin Mohamed bin Zayed Al Nahyan. India played a key role in building consensus, with the declaration expressing serious concerns over attacks on civilian infrastructure and IAEA-safeguarded nuclear facilities, backing India's call for zero tolerance on terrorism including condemnation of the 2025 Pahalgam attack that killed 26 people, and seeking mechanisms to extradite fugitives and recover assets.

#### Global South must shape, not just follow, rules: PM

**In short:** Opening the 18th Brics leaders' summit, PM Narendra Modi said UN Security Council reforms cannot be delayed and the "pyramid of privilege" in global governance must become a "platform of partnership," proposing that Brics draft 10 proposals for global governance reform. He said the Global South "stands in the front row of global crises, but in the back row of decision-making" and urged the bloc to turn developing countries from rule-takers to rule-shapers through economic integration.

#### SIR NOTICE? HERE IS WHAT YOU NEED TO KNOW

**In short:** The Special Intensive Revision claims and notices period from Aug 24 to Oct 22 covers 43.8 lakh voters asked to provide documents establishing place and date of birth, with 7 lakh people having completed hearings by Friday; Pearl D'Souza outlines nine case studies of common verification problems and acceptable documents. Cases include missing 2002 enumeration details resolved with electoral-roll extracts, Aadhaar and passports; father-name mismatches addressed with markscards, birth certificates and family passports; maiden-name changes proved with marriage records; and misspelt names in the 2002 roll confirmed through passport and Aadhaar.

#### Hyderabad Ganesh pandals urged to sing Vande Mataram

**In short:** Chaturthi pandals across Hyderabad are being asked to add a new ritual to this year's festivities — the complete six-stanza Vande Mataram. The Bhagyanagar Utsav Samiti has made the call to nearly 1.4 lakh big and small pandals, tying it to the song's 150th anniversary and Telangana Integration Day on Sept 17, and Ganapati pandal organisers have also been encouraged to hoist the national flag wherever possible.

#### Brics calls for uninterrupted energy flow, infra protection

**In short:** Amid disruptions to global energy supplies, Brics leaders have called for uninterrupted flows of energy from diverse sources and stronger protection of critical energy infrastructure, including cross-border infrastructure, while stressing the continuing role of fossil fuels in the global energy mix. The declaration said energy security was a "crucial foundation for social and economic development, national security and the welfare of all nations," highlighted the need to enhance energy security by ensuring energy market stability and maintaining undisrupted flows from diverse sources, and recognised the continuing role of hydrocarbons while backing greater cooperation on smart grids, energy storage and critical minerals; the emphasis comes amid heightened risks around the Strait of Hormuz and possible disruption of Bab el-Mandeb by Iran-backed Houthis.

#### ECONOMIC POWER OF BRICS

**In short:** World Bank data for 2025 show Brics members' GDP at $19,498 billion (China), $3,956 billion (India), $2,561 billion (Russia), $2,280 billion (Brazil), $1,446 billion (Indonesia), $1,277 billion (Saudi Arabia), $552 billion (UAE), $475 billion (Iran), $427 billion (South Africa), $365 billion (Egypt) and $126 billion (Ethiopia), with per capita GDP (PPP$) ranging from $79,344 (UAE) and $73,784 (Saudi Arabia) to $3,622 (Ethiopia); poverty head count ratios at $3 per day in 2021 PPP include 0% (China), 5.3% (India), 17.4% (South Africa) and 38.6% (Ethiopia). China and India together command nearly 70% of the total Brics economic weight in PPP terms; GDP and per capita GDP figures for UAE and Iran are for 2024.

#### In denouncement of western bloc, Brics hits out at unilateral sanctions and tariff

**In short:** Brics slammed unilateral sanctions and tariff and non-tariff measures initiated by countries, arguing they distorted trade and were inconsistent with global trade rules — a statement seen aimed at the US, where Donald Trump has resorted to raising import duties including reciprocal tariffs declared illegal by the US Supreme Court, with the US still maintaining 10–12.5% tariff on over 50 nations using section 301 powers. The New Delhi Declaration also raised concern over "unilateral sanctions and secondary sanctions" amid US threats over oil purchases from Russia, Iran or Venezuela, noting India faced 50% additional American tariff including 25% "secondary tariffs" for purchasing Russian crude, and hit out at protectionism under the guise of environmental objectives; it backed practical solutions for cross-border payments among Brics countries, reform of World Bank and IMF, development-oriented WTO agriculture negotiations, and expressed strong condemnation of the Pahalgam terrorist attack while calling for halting policies aimed at forced displacement of Palestinians from Gaza despite the Oct 2025 ceasefire agreement.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** In denouncement of western bloc, Brics hits out at unilateral sanctions and tariff
- **Told:** Brics slammed unilateral sanctions and tariff and non-tariff measures initiated by countries, arguing they distorted trade and were inconsistent with global trade rules — a statement seen aimed at the US, where Donald Trump has resorted to raising import duties including reciprocal tariffs declared illegal by the US Supreme Court, with the US still maintaining 10–12.5% tariff on over 50 nations using section 301 powers.

#### Brics opposes trade steps like EU carbon border tax

**In short:** Brics nations Saturday opposed unilateral trade measures such as the EU's carbon border adjustment mechanism (CBAM), which entered its operational regime on Jan 1 and imposes a border tax on carbon-intensive goods like iron and steel, aluminium and cement entering the 27 EU nations, saying such punitive, discriminatory and protectionist measures undermine developing countries' climate efforts and are not in line with international law. The New Delhi Declaration recognised the need to reduce greenhouse gas emissions while seeking to delink unilateral punitive action from climate goals, calling for actions based on equity and common but differentiated responsibilities, and highlighted the need to maintain undisrupted flows of energy from diverse sources including fossil fuels — four Brics members, Russia, Saudi Arabia, Iran and UAE, are among major global exporters of fossil fuel.

#### Summit success gives BJP a boost

**In short:** India’s success in getting BRICS countries with conflicting outlooks—including on West Asia—to back a joint declaration comes as a timely domestic boost for BJP ahead of a month-long public-connect campaign from PM Modi’s birthday on Sept 17. BJP president Nitin Nabin said Bharat has again demonstrated commitment to a more representative world order, declaring “Bharat Leads BRICS 2026” and that the Global South gains a stronger voice.

#### Caste identity alone can't invoke SC/ST Act: HC

**In short:** Allahabad HC held that Scheduled Caste or Scheduled Tribe status alone cannot attract SC/ST Act provisions unless all essential ingredients of the offence are proved, quashing SC/ST charges against Raju Kuraihi and four others in a Ghaziabad property-sale dispute where the Act was invoked solely because the complainant was SC. Justice Santosh Rai said the Loni police FIR and case diary showed no caste-based words or humiliation on account of the victim’s SC/ST status.

#### FSSAI orders recall of coriander powder, carrageenan made by 2 food operators

**In short:** FSSAI ordered immediate recall of “Shalimar’s Chef – Coriander Powder” (M/s Nilgiri Oil & Allied Industries) and “Carrageenan” (M/s Aquagri Processing Pvt Ltd) after lab tests found them non-compliant. Coriander powder failed on pesticide residues, remained non-compliant on retest after appeal, and was deemed “unsafe” under the Food Safety and Standards Act, 2006; carrageenan failed on prescribed food additives and was also opined “unsafe” by the referral laboratory.

#### Five killed as van plunges into ditch

**In short:** Five people died when their van collided with an Arunachal Pradesh-registered truck on NH-15 at Tubukijhar in Assam’s Sonitpur district around midnight Friday; the van tumbled into a roadside ditch, was mangled beyond recognition and occupants were trapped until rescue teams extricated the bodies. The truck driver fled but was arrested later.

#### Jarange's 5-day Mum march to clash with Chauti

**In short:** Rejecting Maharashtra govt appeals to defer protests during Ganesh Chaturthi, Maratha quota activist Manoj Jarange announced a five-day march from Antarwali Sarati (Jalna) to Mumbai’s Azad Maidan for an indefinite hunger strike from Sept 20, with a first halt at Beed’s Patoda on Sept 16—suggesting a Sept 15 start and ~100 km daily legs. The march is expected to overlap the 10-day Ganesh festival from Sept 14; Jarange urged non-violent, disciplined participation with no stone-pelting or arson.

#### Delayed Tax Payment Alone Not Wilful Evasion / HC junks tax evasion case against fashion designers

**In short:** Bombay HC on Sept 10 quashed I-T criminal proceedings against Abu Jani, Sandeep Khosla and their Andheri-based company over delayed 2017-18 tax payment, holding “mere failure to pay or delayed payment cannot be equated to wilful evasion.” The firm declared Rs 15.2 lakh income; Rs 5.27 lakh tax was payable after deductions. It paid Rs 5.27 lakh three days after an Oct 16, 2018 show-cause notice; I-T later sought Rs 1 lakh interest and filed a Sept 2019 complaint under sections 276C(2) and 278B.

Bombay HC on Sept 10 quashed I-T criminal proceedings against Abu Jani, Sandeep Khosla and their Andheri-based company over delayed 2017-18 tax payment, holding “mere failure to pay or delayed payment cannot be equated to wilful evasion.” The firm declared Rs 15.2 lakh income; Rs 5.27 lakh tax was payable after deductions. It paid Rs 5.27 lakh three days after an Oct 16, 2018 show-cause notice; I-T later sought Rs 1 lakh interest and filed a Sept 2019 complaint under sections 276C(2) and 278B. Justice Ranjitsinha Bhosale said continuing the case would be “a manifest abuse of the process of law.”

#### 'Reaction to a fall is not kissing': Man gets 1-yr jail

**In short:** A Mumbai sessions court sentenced 34-year-old cleaner Ritesh Armulla to one year’s rigorous imprisonment for forcibly kissing a Borivli (W) mall saleswoman twice on Sept 17, 2025, rejecting his claim he accidentally slipped and fell onto her. The court held a natural reaction to falling would not involve kissing, finding him guilty of criminal force and unwelcome physical contact with explicit sexual overtones after a two-month trial.

#### Man pushes wife to death from terrace over dowry, held

**In short:** Noida police arrested a Muradnagar man for allegedly pushing his wife Akriti to death from their two-storey home terrace over unmet dowry demands of Rs 30 lakh and a car; four other family members are yet to be arrested. Akriti, a Bhangel resident, had married Gaurav about six months ago after a Feb 20 wedding was called off over extra dowry demands and a later police-station compromise; on Sept 7 night she called her brother alleging assault and fearing her in-laws might kill her.

#### IPO needs majority nod from nominee directors

**In short:** RBI’s Sept 11 letter on Tata Sons’ IPO continuation is expected at the Sept 17 board meeting, upending Tata Trusts chairman Noel Tata’s effort to keep the company private and strengthening Shapoorji Pallonji Group chairman Shapoor Mistry (18.4% stake), who has pushed for listing. Under Article 121A, an IPO needs majority approval from Tata Trusts nominees Noel Tata and Venu Srinivasan; with Srinivasan publicly backing listing and Noel opposed, a 1-1 tie would leave Tata Sons chairman with the casting vote.

RBI’s Sept 11 letter on Tata Sons’ IPO continuation is expected at the Sept 17 board meeting, upending Tata Trusts chairman Noel Tata’s effort to keep the company private and strengthening Shapoorji Pallonji Group chairman Shapoor Mistry (18.4% stake), who has pushed for listing. Under Article 121A, an IPO needs majority approval from Tata Trusts nominees Noel Tata and Venu Srinivasan; with Srinivasan publicly backing listing and Noel opposed, a 1-1 tie would leave Tata Sons chairman with the casting vote. Sir Ratan Tata Trust (24%) is under regulatory ban; Sir Dorabji Tata Trust (28%) must act jointly with SRTT. The board may also ask chairman N Chandrasekaran to reconsider stepping down when his term ends Feb 20, 2027.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** IPO needs majority nod from nominee directors
- **Told:** RBI’s Sept 11 letter on Tata Sons’ IPO continuation is expected at the Sept 17 board meeting, upending Tata Trusts chairman Noel Tata’s effort to keep the company private and strengthening Shapoorji Pallonji Group chairman Shapoor Mistry (18.4% stake), who has pushed for listing.
- **Purpose:** The board may also ask chairman N Chandrasekaran to reconsider stepping down when his term ends Feb 20, 2027.

**Important points**
- Sir Ratan Tata Trust (24%) is under regulatory ban.
- Sir Dorabji Tata Trust (28%) must act jointly with SRTT.
- The board may also ask chairman N Chandrasekaran to reconsider stepping down when his term ends Feb 20, 2027.
_Sources: Times of India Bengaluru p.18_

#### You shouldn't think you are someone super special all the time, it can be damaging: Saif

**In short:** In a continued Bombay Times Lounge interview as Haiwaan releases in theatres, Saif Ali Khan said his visually impaired Haiwaan character had to stay subtle because special abilities meant he did not read as handicapped, drawing partly on blind-swordsman tropes from Japanese samurai films. He said fame means accepting recognition while not treating stardom as all-defining at home, and warned against believing you are “super special” all the time.

In a continued Bombay Times Lounge interview as Haiwaan releases in theatres, Saif Ali Khan said his visually impaired Haiwaan character had to stay subtle because special abilities meant he did not read as handicapped, drawing partly on blind-swordsman tropes from Japanese samurai films. He said fame means accepting recognition while not treating stardom as all-defining at home, and warned against believing you are “super special” all the time. On parenting Sara, Ibrahim, Taimur and Jehangir across Millennial, Gen Z and Gen Alpha cohorts, he said children’s simplicity refreshes adult thinking, older kids offer their own lessons, and spending one-on-one time with each child reveals them differently from group dynamics.

### International

#### BRICS meet calls for max restraint in West Asia

**In short:** The BRICS on Saturday called for maximum restraint in West Asia, expressed serious concern over deliberate attacks on civilian infrastructure and peaceful IAEA-safeguarded nuclear facilities, and urged respect for sovereignty and territorial integrity without assigning blame, as diplomats bridged differences between Iran and Saudi Arabia and the UAE overnight to adopt the New Delhi Declaration at the 18th summit hosted by Prime Minister Narendra Modi at Bharat Mandapam. On Israel-Palestine, BRICS invoked ICJ proceedings, reaffirmed support for Palestinian rights and a two-state solution based on 1967 borders, and Modi said the declaration provides clear direction that must be translated into tangible results.

The BRICS on Saturday called for maximum restraint in West Asia, expressed serious concern over deliberate attacks on civilian infrastructure and peaceful IAEA-safeguarded nuclear facilities, and urged respect for sovereignty and territorial integrity without assigning blame, as diplomats bridged differences between Iran and Saudi Arabia and the UAE overnight to adopt the New Delhi Declaration at the 18th summit hosted by Prime Minister Narendra Modi at Bharat Mandapam. On Israel-Palestine, BRICS invoked ICJ proceedings, reaffirmed support for Palestinian rights and a two-state solution based on 1967 borders, and Modi said the declaration provides clear direction that must be translated into tangible results.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** BRICS meet calls for max restraint in West Asia
- **Told:** The BRICS on Saturday called for maximum restraint in West Asia, expressed serious concern over deliberate attacks on civilian infrastructure and peaceful IAEA-safeguarded nuclear facilities, and urged respect for sovereignty and territorial integrity without assigning blame, as diplomats bridged differences between Iran and Saudi Arabia and the UAE overnight to adopt the New Delhi Declaration at the 18th summit hosted by Prime Minister Narendra Modi at Bharat Mandapam.
- **Purpose:** On Israel-Palestine, BRICS invoked ICJ proceedings, reaffirmed support for Palestinian rights and a two-state solution based on 1967 borders, and Modi said the declaration provides clear direction that must be translated into tangible results.

**Important points**
- BRICS invoked ICJ proceedings, reaffirmed support for Palestinian rights and a two-state solution based on 1967 borders, and Modi said the declaration provides clear direction that must be translated into tangible results.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.1; Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.7_

#### BRICS countries adopt New Delhi Declaration

**In short:** The New Delhi declaration called for comprehensive reform of the United Nations, including support for Brazil and India to get a bigger role in the Security Council, and expressed deep concern over attacks on civilian infrastructure and nuclear facilities in West Asia. Prime Minister Narendra Modi said BRICS was "coming of age" with a distinct identity not aimed against others, proposed a troika system like the G-20, a road map for global governance reform, a seafarer distress network, and a BRICS global south mechanism for skill development in new technologies.

The New Delhi declaration called for comprehensive reform of the United Nations, including support for Brazil and India to get a bigger role in the Security Council, and expressed deep concern over attacks on civilian infrastructure and nuclear facilities in West Asia. Prime Minister Narendra Modi said BRICS was "coming of age" with a distinct identity not aimed against others, proposed a troika system like the G-20, a road map for global governance reform, a seafarer distress network, and a BRICS global south mechanism for skill development in new technologies. Chinese President Xi Jinping, who held an hour-long bilateral with Mr. Modi after the session, announced China would take over the BRICS presidency for the next year; the declaration also criticised the U.S. blockade of Cuba and expressed concern over violence in Sudan. Consensus on West Asia language was achieved through months of threadbare discussions after Iran sought strong condemnation of U.S. and Israel strikes and the UAE wanted equal condemnation of Iran's attacks, with Indian Sherpa Sudhakar Dalela saying cooperation of all members was key; BRICS countries also underscored consensus against cross-border terrorism, with specific reference to the Pahalgam terror attack in 2025.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** BRICS countries adopt New Delhi Declaration
- **Told:** The New Delhi declaration called for comprehensive reform of the United Nations, including support for Brazil and India to get a bigger role in the Security Council, and expressed deep concern over attacks on civilian infrastructure and nuclear facilities in West Asia.
- **Purpose:** and Israel strikes and the UAE wanted equal condemnation of Iran's attacks, with Indian Sherpa Sudhakar Dalela saying cooperation of all members was key; BRICS countries also underscored consensus against cross-border terrorism, with specific reference to the Pahalgam terror attack in 2025.

**Important points**
- Chinese President Xi Jinping, who held an hour-long bilateral with Mr.
- Modi after the session, announced China would take over the BRICS presidency for the next year.
- the declaration also criticised the U.S.
- blockade of Cuba and expressed concern over violence in Sudan.
- Consensus on West Asia language was achieved through months of threadbare discussions after Iran sought strong condemnation of U.S.
- and Israel strikes and the UAE wanted equal condemnation of Iran's attacks, with Indian Sherpa Sudhakar Dalela saying cooperation of all members was key.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.1; News Trail Mangaluru p.1; The Hindu Mangaluru p.1; The Hindu Mangaluru p.7; The Hindu Mangaluru p.8_

#### Saudi Arabia closes key oil pipeline after strikes

**In short:** Threatening to send energy prices even higher amid the war in West Asia, Saudi Arabia has shut down its 1,200-kilometre-long East-West pipeline after the vital oil conduit came under aerial attack, with the kingdom saying the attack originated in Iraq where Iran-backed militias operate. The pipeline, used by Riyadh as an alternative to the Strait of Hormuz, has been moving close to 5 million barrels of oil per day in recent months, amounting to 5% of global supply.

Threatening to send energy prices even higher amid the war in West Asia, Saudi Arabia has shut down its 1,200-kilometre-long East-West pipeline after the vital oil conduit came under aerial attack, with the kingdom saying the attack originated in Iraq where Iran-backed militias operate. The pipeline, used by Riyadh as an alternative to the Strait of Hormuz, has been moving close to 5 million barrels of oil per day in recent months, amounting to 5% of global supply. The Saudi Arabian Foreign Ministry said the attack resulted in some injuries and damage that was being assessed; though an umbrella group of Iran-backed militias in Iraq denied involvement, Iraq's government said the attacks originated from its territory, pledged a probe, and dismissed two senior officials, AP reported.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Saudi Arabia closes key oil pipeline after strikes
- **Told:** Threatening to send energy prices even higher amid the war in West Asia, Saudi Arabia has shut down its 1,200-kilometre-long East-West pipeline after the vital oil conduit came under aerial attack, with the kingdom saying the attack originated in Iraq where Iran-backed militias operate.
- **Purpose:** The Saudi Arabian Foreign Ministry said the attack resulted in some injuries and damage that was being assessed; though an umbrella group of Iran-backed militias in Iraq denied involvement, Iraq's government said the attacks originated from its territory, pledged a probe, and dismissed two senior officials, AP reported.

**Important points**
- The Saudi Arabian Foreign Ministry said the attack resulted in some injuries and damage that was being assessed.
- though an umbrella group of Iran-backed militias in Iraq denied involvement, Iraq's government said the attacks originated from its territory, pledged a probe, and dismissed two senior officials, AP reported.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.10; News Trail Mangaluru p.1; The Hindu Mangaluru p.1; The Hindu Mangaluru p.13; Times of India Bengaluru p.2_

#### Trump stirs Irish stew, risks souring British ties

**In short:** During a visit to Ireland on Saturday, President Donald Trump told Taoiseach Micheal Martin he would "love to see" Irish reunification happen now rather than eventually, calling it "a fantastic thing" while noting "The UK will have something to say about it, obviously." The remarks sent ripples through Northern Ireland, where unionist politicians stressed the province's constitutional future is for its people to decide under the Good Friday Agreement, and analysts warned similar unpredictable pronouncements could unsettle diplomacy on issues from Korea to Taiwan.

#### England complete 3-0 sweep of Pak

**In short:** England beat Pakistan by eight wickets at Edgbaston on Saturday to seal a 3-0 Test series sweep, chasing 130 after losing both openers in the first over to Mohammad Abbas. Joe Root was unbeaten on 62 and Jordan Cox 59 not out, though England had floundered on Friday when debutant Razaullah raced to 81 off 63 balls with nine sixes and added 121 for the ninth wicket with Abbas (42); Pakistan had already lost the first two Tests by an innings and 103 runs and by 194 runs, sacked coaches Sarfaraz Ahmed and Umar Gul, and sent seven players home after Lord’s.

#### Sevilla rub salt into Valencia wounds with 1-0 win

#### Revisiting the feminist icon

**In short:** Aisiri Amin recalls Gloria Steinem’s death at 92, tracing her Ohio childhood, two years in India on the Charles Bowles Fellowship including time at Delhi University’s Miranda House, 1963 undercover reporting on Hugh Hefner’s Playboy Club, co-founding *Ms Magazine* in 1971, and landmark feminist advocacy. Amin also notes criticism that Steinem catered to educated white women, her CIA-linked Independent Research Service work, 1998 defence of Bill Clinton, evolving 2013 support for transgender people, and her 1958 sari advertisement image from the Gloria Steinem Foundation archives.

#### Courage to refuse

**In short:** Chintan Girish Modi profiles Ella Keidar Greenberg, imprisoned in March 2025 as the first openly transgender conscientious objector in Israel since Hamas’s October 7, 2023 attack, and Aiden Katri, who refused enlistment in 2016 citing apartheid against Palestinians. He links their “refusenik” tradition to resistance against compulsory military service and “pinkwashing,” quoting Ella’s pledge that she would tell future grandchildren she chose to resist during the genocide.

#### BRICS bold stand: 'Enough' in Mideast

**In short:** The BRICS group unanimously adopted a joint declaration at its two-day New Delhi summit hosted by PM Modi, calling for calm in the Middle East and stressing cooperation to maintain global trade, supply chains and energy flows. The declaration voiced deep concern over escalating tensions, called for maximum restraint, warned against attacks on civilian infrastructure and safeguarded nuclear facilities, and reiterated support for Palestine including full UN membership.

#### Iran President, Abu Dhabi Crown Prince discuss regional hostilities

**In short:** Against the backdrop of continued hostilities in West Asia, Iranian President Masoud Pezeshkian and Abu Dhabi Crown Prince Sheikh Khaled bin Mohamed bin Zayed Al Nahyan held talks in New Delhi on Saturday on the sidelines of the 18th BRICS Summit, discussing regional hostilities and expressing support for de-escalation. The conversation came ten days after Iran targeted military installations in the United Arab Emirates; the New Delhi Declaration expressed deep concern about multiple West Asia conflicts but stopped short of naming major stakeholders.

Against the backdrop of continued hostilities in West Asia, Iranian President Masoud Pezeshkian and Abu Dhabi Crown Prince Sheikh Khaled bin Mohamed bin Zayed Al Nahyan held talks in New Delhi on Saturday on the sidelines of the 18th BRICS Summit, discussing regional hostilities and expressing support for de-escalation. The conversation came ten days after Iran targeted military installations in the United Arab Emirates; the New Delhi Declaration expressed deep concern about multiple West Asia conflicts but stopped short of naming major stakeholders. The Abu Dhabi Media Office said both leaders underscored the importance of easing tensions, promoting de-escalation and strengthening regional stability, while Iranian state outlet IRNA said the meeting followed Iran's emphasis on strengthening relations with its southern neighbours. Mr. Pezeshkian said West Asia does not need the United States, at war with Iran, to be a regional policeman, telling Malaysian Prime Minister Anwar Ibrahim that the region's territorial integrity and security can only be guaranteed by its main players. The declaration called for maximum restraint, maintaining smooth global trade and energy flows, halting policies aimed at forced displacement of Palestinians, support for a sovereign Palestinian state within 1967 borders with East Jerusalem as capital, Israel's obligation to supply humanitarian aid in Gaza, withdrawal of Israeli forces from Lebanese territories, and took a strong position on attacks on UNIFIL.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Iran President, Abu Dhabi Crown Prince discuss regional hostilities
- **Told:** Against the backdrop of continued hostilities in West Asia, Iranian President Masoud Pezeshkian and Abu Dhabi Crown Prince Sheikh Khaled bin Mohamed bin Zayed Al Nahyan held talks in New Delhi on Saturday on the sidelines of the 18th BRICS Summit, discussing regional hostilities and expressing support for de-escalation.
- **Purpose:** The declaration called for maximum restraint, maintaining smooth global trade and energy flows, halting policies aimed at forced displacement of Palestinians, support for a sovereign Palestinian state within 1967 borders with East Jerusalem as capital, Israel's obligation to supply humanitarian aid in Gaza, withdrawal of Israeli forces from Lebanese territories, and took a strong position on attacks on UNIFIL.

**Important points**
- The Abu Dhabi Media Office said both leaders underscored the importance of easing tensions, promoting de-escalation and strengthening regional stability, while Iranian state outlet IRNA said the meeting followed Iran's emphasis on strengthening relations with its southern neighbours.
- Mr.
- Pezeshkian said West Asia does not need the United States, at war with Iran, to be a regional policeman, telling Malaysian Prime Minister Anwar Ibrahim that the region's territorial integrity and security can only be guaranteed by its main players.
- The declaration called for maximum restraint, maintaining smooth global trade and energy flows, halting policies aimed at forced displacement of Palestinians, support for a sovereign Palestinian state within 1967 borders with East Jerusalem as capital.
- Israel's obligation to supply humanitarian aid in Gaza, withdrawal of Israeli forces from Lebanese territories, and took a strong position on attacks on UNIFIL.
_Sources: The Hindu Mangaluru p.7; Times of India Bengaluru p.3; Times of India Bengaluru p.9_

#### BRICS nations fortify disease monitoring

**In short:** BRICS leaders on Saturday called for stronger cooperation in disease surveillance and medical product regulation, welcoming the establishment of a dedicated sub-working group to advance an integrated early warning system for the prevention and response to mass infectious diseases. In their New Delhi Declaration, the leaders stressed the importance of strengthening health infrastructure and local production of medical products to enhance preparedness for future health emergencies.

BRICS leaders on Saturday called for stronger cooperation in disease surveillance and medical product regulation, welcoming the establishment of a dedicated sub-working group to advance an integrated early warning system for the prevention and response to mass infectious diseases. The initiative is aimed at strengthening collective preparedness against infectious disease threats through closer coordination among member countries.

#### 'Brazil, India forging ties for a changing world'

**In short:** Brazil's Foreign Minister Mauro Vieira praised India's BRICS presidency and said bilateral trade could exceed the $30 billion target well before 2030, adding it is not correct to view BRICS as an anti-West alliance. Following President Lula da Silva's February visit, Brazil and India are rapidly expanding trade past last year's $15 billion toward $20 billion, and Mr.

Brazil's Foreign Minister Mauro Vieira praised India's BRICS presidency and said bilateral trade could exceed the $30 billion target well before 2030, adding it is not correct to view BRICS as an anti-West alliance. Following President Lula da Silva's February visit, Brazil and India are rapidly expanding trade past last year's $15 billion toward $20 billion, and Mr. Vieira said deals by Embraer, Vale, WEG and others could push trade above $30 billion in the short term. He highlighted the New Development Bank as a vital alternative to Bretton Woods institutions, said Xi Jinping and Vladimir Putin's attendance underscores India's pivotal global role, and argued BRICS allows major regional powers with bilateral differences to engage informally; the group has entered a consolidation phase focused on integrating new members rather than further enlargement. Mr. Vieira said Brazil's foreign policy relies on peace, constitutional multilateralism and active diplomacy over military force, with President Lula expanding trade and political ties worldwide.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** 'Brazil, India forging ties for a changing world'
- **Told:** Brazil's Foreign Minister Mauro Vieira praised India's BRICS presidency and said bilateral trade could exceed the $30 billion target well before 2030, adding it is not correct to view BRICS as an anti-West alliance.
- **Purpose:** Vieira said Brazil's foreign policy relies on peace, constitutional multilateralism and active diplomacy over military force, with President Lula expanding trade and political ties worldwide.

**Important points**
- Vieira said deals by Embraer, Vale, WEG and others could push trade above $30 billion in the short term.
- He highlighted the New Development Bank as a vital alternative to Bretton Woods institutions, said Xi Jinping and Vladimir Putin's attendance underscores India's pivotal global role, and argued BRICS allows major regional powers with bilateral differences to engage informally.
- the group has entered a consolidation phase focused on integrating new members rather than further enlargement.
- Vieira said Brazil's foreign policy relies on peace, constitutional multilateralism and active diplomacy over military force, with President Lula expanding trade and political ties worldwide.
_Sources: The Hindu Mangaluru p.7_

#### Human drug use began millennia earlier than believed

**In short:** Researchers examined the teeth of two ancient hunter-gatherers from Sulawesi, Indonesia, dating back as far as 25,000 years, and found unusual deep grooves caused by sucking on betel nuts, with chemical analyses confirming the presence of arecoline, the main stimulant in betel nuts. The find suggests these early foragers habitually kept whole nuts in their mouths for long periods, pushing back the timeline of human drug use by thousands of years.

The find suggests these early foragers habitually kept whole nuts in their mouths for long periods, pushing back the timeline of human drug use by thousands of years.

#### Even Teflon may not stop water if the liquid is charged

**In short:** As water drops slide over leaves, glass or plastic, they develop an electric charge, and when such charged drops land on a metal surface, even one with a protective coating like Teflon, the charge creates an electric field strong enough to punch microscopic holes through the coating, allowing water to reach the metal below and leading to rust and decay. This new discovery shows a previously unrecognised way in which bridges, ships, cars, and heritage sites may degrade.

This new discovery shows a previously unrecognised way in which bridges, ships, cars, and heritage sites may degrade.

#### The new lords of the Red Sea

**In short:** The Houthis seized Perim Island at the mouth of the Bab el-Mandeb Strait in early September after Saudi-backed forces bombed Sana'a airport's runway to prevent an Iranian aircraft carrying a Houthi delegation from landing, breaking a 2022 ceasefire and triggering Houthi attacks on Saudi energy infrastructure including the shutdown of the East-West pipeline. Rooted in the Zaydi revivalist 'Believing Youth' movement founded in the early 1990s, the Houthis captured Sana'a in 2014, survived a Saudi-led bombing campaign from 2015, and have transformed into a formidable territorial and naval force with Iranian support, now controlling the Red Sea coastline and, together with Iran's closure of the Strait of Hormuz, exerting pressure on two of the world's critical maritime chokepoints through which more than 20% of seaborne oil trade and roughly 12-15% of global maritime trade typically pass.

#### BAPS

**In short:** The Eiffel Tower was closed on Monday after its management replaced all women staff with male colleagues for the visit of a 100-strong delegation from the Bochasanwasi Akshar Purushottam Swaminarayan Sanstha, sparking a union protest and an internal investigation by the company that runs the monument, which called the incident "totally shocking and unacceptable". BAPS, a Gujarat-based Swaminarayan denomination with over 1,300 temples worldwide, is known for strict gender segregation and has faced controversy including a 2021 U.S.

The Eiffel Tower was closed on Monday after its management replaced all women staff with male colleagues for the visit of a 100-strong delegation from the Bochasanwasi Akshar Purushottam Swaminarayan Sanstha, sparking a union protest and an internal investigation by the company that runs the monument, which called the incident "totally shocking and unacceptable". BAPS, a Gujarat-based Swaminarayan denomination with over 1,300 temples worldwide, is known for strict gender segregation and has faced controversy including a 2021 U.S. lawsuit alleging exploitation of Indian workers on religious visas, while the visit coincided with the inauguration of a Swaminarayan temple in Bussy-Saint-Georges virtually opened by Prime Minister Narendra Modi as "a new milestone in the cultural relations between India and France".

**What this is**
- **Concept:** BAPS
- **Told:** The Eiffel Tower was closed on Monday after its management replaced all women staff with male colleagues for the visit of a 100-strong delegation from the Bochasanwasi Akshar Purushottam Swaminarayan Sanstha, sparking a union protest and an internal investigation by the company that runs the monument, which called the incident "totally shocking and unacceptable".
- **Purpose:** lawsuit alleging exploitation of Indian workers on religious visas, while the visit coincided with the inauguration of a Swaminarayan temple in Bussy-Saint-Georges virtually opened by Prime Minister Narendra Modi as "a new milestone in the cultural relations between India and France".

**Important points**
- lawsuit alleging exploitation of Indian workers on religious visas, while the visit coincided with the inauguration of a Swaminarayan temple in Bussy-Saint-Georges virtually opened by Prime Minister Narendra Modi as "a new milestone in the cultural relations between India and France".

#### Alice Weidel

**In short:** Alice Weidel, co-leader of Germany's far-right Alternative für Deutschland, declared "Mr. Merz, your time has run out" after the party secured 43% of the vote in Saxony Anhalt, consolidating its position as a front-runner ahead of the 2029 federal elections.

Alice Weidel, co-leader of Germany's far-right Alternative für Deutschland, declared "Mr. Merz, your time has run out" after the party secured 43% of the vote in Saxony Anhalt, consolidating its position as a front-runner ahead of the 2029 federal elections. A former Goldman Sachs analyst fluent in Mandarin who lived in China for nearly six years, Ms. Weidel is the AfD's first lesbian leader and is in a civil partnership with a German national of Sri Lankan origin with two adopted children, yet the party opposes same-sex marriage and emphasises Christian values and traditional families while campaigning against immigration from Muslim-majority countries. Her grandfather Hans Weidel was a Nazi party member and military judge, and Ms. Weidel has dismissed Germany's culture of remembrance over Nazi crimes as a "cult of guilt" even as she gains favour with a wider German electorate.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Alice Weidel
- **Told:** Alice Weidel, co-leader of Germany's far-right Alternative für Deutschland, declared "Mr.
- **Purpose:** Weidel has dismissed Germany's culture of remembrance over Nazi crimes as a "cult of guilt" even as she gains favour with a wider German electorate.

- Weidel is the AfD's first lesbian leader and is in a civil partnership with a German national of Sri Lankan origin with two adopted children, yet the party opposes same-sex marriage and emphasises Christian values and traditional families while campaigning against immigration from Muslim-majority countries.
- Her grandfather Hans Weidel was a Nazi party member and military judge, and Ms.

#### 5 killed, dozens injured in Russian strikes on Ukraine

**In short:** Russian strikes killed five civilians and wounded dozens in Ukraine on Saturday, local officials said, after Russian President Vladimir Putin warned that sending European troops into Ukraine would amount to direct conflict with Russia. Moscow's intensifying air campaign targeted Ukraine's power grid ahead of winter, with attacks on Zaporizhzhia, Kryvyi Rih, Odesa and Zhytomyr region killing civilians and destroying a residential high-rise in Odesa where 35 people were wounded in what regional head Oleh Kiper called a "massive attack".

#### Uganda buries 'youngest' king

**In short:** Several thousand people gathered on Saturday in Uganda to pay final respects to King Oyo Nyimba Kabamba Iguru Rukidi IV, who ascended the throne at age three and reigned over the Tooro Kingdom for 31 years before dying at 34 while receiving cancer treatment in the United States. Guinness World Records had recognised him as the world's youngest reigning monarch, and President urged unity amid a dispute over succession at a burial ceremony at Saint John's Cathedral in Fort Portal, where his sister Princess Ruth Komuntale said he "had a gift to make people laugh."

#### It's an all-American Top 10 rich list as tech dominates

**In short:** French luxury goods tycoon Bernard Arnault fell out of the top 10 on the Bloomberg Billionaires Index as his net worth dropped to $143 billion, leaving an all-American roster of mostly tech billionaires to dominate the rankings for the first time since the index's 2012 launch. Elon Musk leads with a $918.8 billion fortune, followed by Larry Page, Jeff Bezos, Sergey Brin and Michael Dell, while Arnault fell below Warren Buffett's $143 billion.

#### Al-Qaida, terror training camps back in Af: US

**In short:** Al-Qaida and terrorist training camps are back in Afghanistan 25 years after 9/11, with the Washington Post reporting at least eight training sites established over the past three years exploiting the security vacuum after the Taliban's return and the 2021 US withdrawal. UN monitors and Western intelligence officials say the camps cover weapons, explosives, guerrilla warfare and instruction involving encryption, drones and AI, with militant Islamist groups now present in at least 14 of Afghanistan's 34 provinces; the report notes al-Qaida's current leader Saif al-Adel is believed to be hiding in Iran and revisits Pakistan's historical role in the network's South Asia operations.

#### Capgemini sells subsidiary after row over ICE ties

**In short:** French IT giant Capgemini said on Saturday it was selling its US subsidiary Capgemini Government Solutions to ITC Federal after the unit came under pressure for its work for US Immigration and Customs Enforcement amid nationwide backlash over ICE's aggressive tactics in enforcing President Donald Trump's immigration crackdown. The subsidiary represented 0.4% of the group's global 2025 revenue and less than 2% of its US revenue.

#### Zelensky ready to meet Putin at G20

**In short:** Ukrainian President Volodymyr Zelensky said he would be willing to meet Russian leader Vladimir Putin at the G20 summit in Miami on Dec 14-15 for talks on ending more than four years of war, telling Deutsche Welle that leaders "have to meet, to speak, and make decisions" because only "results" matter. The Kremlin said it had not decided whether Putin would attend and repeated his demand that Zelensky come to Russia for talks, as US negotiators visit Kyiv and Moscow to restart stalled peace efforts.

#### Toll in Philippines ferry fire rises to 76; 13 missing

**In short:** Philippine rescuers recovered 41 more charred remains from a ferry fire on Saturday, raising the death toll to 76 with 13 people unaccounted for after the vessel caught fire on Wednesday en route from Manila to Palawan. Forensic teams set up temporary tents in Coron town to identify victims, with the Coast Guard finding burnt remains in the economy accommodation section and the Maritime Industry Authority citing survivor accounts of two explosions shortly before the fire broke out.

#### Tunisia reports prison deaths due to heatwave

**In short:** At least 23 prisoners have died in Tunisia in recent weeks due to extreme heat, according to rights advocates, though authorities have not released official summer prison-death figures and said no death can be attributed to heat without forensic confirmation. The Tunisian League for Human Rights documented the 23 deaths based on information from doctors, lawyers and relatives of prisoners who died in jail from the heat.

#### Egyptians budget for bread after mass food aid cuts

**In short:** Eman Abdel Fattah, a 42-year-old Cairo mother of four, said her family now worries about affording bread after their food subsidy card was suspended in July, with flatbread costing 10 times more than before. She is among 850,000 people removed from Egypt's subsidy system since June as part of IMF-backed reforms that will reportedly exclude millions more next year, in a country where a third of Egyptians lived below the poverty line before the 2022 economic crisis.

#### Chinese readout quotes Modi as saying ties with Beijing not influenced by any country

**In short:** PM Modi and Chinese President Xi Jinping held a 40-minute bilateral on the BRICS summit margins in New Delhi—their third meeting in two years after Kazan (2024) and Tianjin, and the first on Indian soil in seven years. India’s readout cited commitment to border resolution and addressing trade deficit and supply-chain concerns; a Chinese readout quoted Modi as saying India follows an independent foreign policy and ties with Beijing are not influenced by any third country, and that India’s Taiwan and Tibet positions are unchanged.

PM Modi and Chinese President Xi Jinping held a 40-minute bilateral on the BRICS summit margins in New Delhi—their third meeting in two years after Kazan (2024) and Tianjin, and the first on Indian soil in seven years. India’s readout cited commitment to border resolution and addressing trade deficit and supply-chain concerns; a Chinese readout quoted Modi as saying India follows an independent foreign policy and ties with Beijing are not influenced by any third country, and that India’s Taiwan and Tibet positions are unchanged. Beijing had attributed a similar remark to S Jaishankar in July; MEA responded that China has illegally occupied Indian territory in J&K and Ladakh since 1963.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Chinese readout quotes Modi as saying ties with Beijing not influenced by any country
- **Told:** PM Modi and Chinese President Xi Jinping held a 40-minute bilateral on the BRICS summit margins in New Delhi—their third meeting in two years after Kazan (2024) and Tianjin, and the first on Indian soil in seven years.
- **Purpose:** Beijing had attributed a similar remark to S Jaishankar in July; MEA responded that China has illegally occupied Indian territory in J&K and Ladakh since 1963.

#### Modi proposes emergency seafarers support network

**In short:** Addressing the BRICS summit, PM Modi called for UNSC reform with text-based negotiations tied to a fixed timeline, greater voting power for growth-driving economies in global financial institutions, and a BRICS Continuity and Implementation Mechanism via the troika to sustain institutional momentum across rotating presidencies. He proposed a Seafarers Emergency Support Network linking maritime authorities and missions for distress alerts, medical aid, family notifications and evacuations, and said the Global South must move from “rule-takers to rule-shapers.” Modi said BRICS operates with an inclusive approach, not against anyone, as the bloc marks 20 years on the global stage.

#### Don't agree with all his views, says Anwar Ibrahim on eve of meet with Kerala cleric

**In short:** Malaysian PM Anwar Ibrahim, ahead of a Sunday visit to Kozhikode to meet All-India Sunni Jemiyyathul Ulema general secretary Kanthapuram A P Aboobacker Musaliyar, told NDTV he does not agree with every view of the cleric, whose recent call for women to stay confined to homes drew backlash. Ibrahim called Kanthapuram a traditional Islamic scholar who also runs a college for women, but said in Malaysia women have equal rights, with more female university students and his wife a doctor.

Malaysian PM Anwar Ibrahim, ahead of a Sunday visit to Kozhikode to meet All-India Sunni Jemiyyathul Ulema general secretary Kanthapuram A P Aboobacker Musaliyar, told NDTV he does not agree with every view of the cleric, whose recent call for women to stay confined to homes drew backlash. Ibrahim called Kanthapuram a traditional Islamic scholar who also runs a college for women, but said in Malaysia women have equal rights, with more female university students and his wife a doctor. This is Ibrahim’s second visit to Kanthapuram’s Markaz campus after Sept 2022; Malaysia conferred its Hijra Award on Kanthapuram in 2023.

#### Snoop to make VMAs comeback

**In short:** Snoop Dogg will host the 2026 Video Music Awards at Peacock Theatre in Los Angeles on September 27, marking the ceremony’s return to the West Coast since 2017. The rapper, nominated 13 times and a three-time winner who debuted at the VMAs in 1994, said hosting completes a “full-circle moment” after MTV helped give hip hop “a place to be fearless and seen by the whole world.”

#### Lady Gaga welcomes first child with Michael Polansky

**In short:** Pop superstar Lady Gaga has welcomed her first child with fiancé Michael Polansky, US media reports said. The 40-year-old singer, whose real name is Stefani Germanotta, began dating the entrepreneur in 2020 and got engaged in 2024; sources confirmed the baby’s arrival but did not disclose the child’s name, sex or date of birth.

#### Diana's iconic revenge dress is up for auction

**In short:** Princess Diana’s black silk “revenge dress” will be auctioned at Sotheby’s New York on December 9, with an estimate of $150,000 to $300,000 (₹1.4 to 2.8 crore). Designed by Christina Stambolian, the off-the-shoulder gown was worn to a Vanity Fair gala in London on June 29, 1994—the same evening King Charles III, then her husband, publicly admitted to infidelity—and became a symbol of her confidence and independence.

Princess Diana’s black silk “revenge dress” will be auctioned at Sotheby’s New York on December 9, with an estimate of $150,000 to $300,000 (₹1.4 to 2.8 crore). Designed by Christina Stambolian, the off-the-shoulder gown was worn to a Vanity Fair gala in London on June 29, 1994—the same evening King Charles III, then her husband, publicly admitted to infidelity—and became a symbol of her confidence and independence. Last sold in 1997 for charity, the dress will be displayed in London before touring Hong Kong and Geneva.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Diana's iconic revenge dress is up for auction
- **Told:** Princess Diana’s black silk “revenge dress” will be auctioned at Sotheby’s New York on December 9, with an estimate of $150,000 to $300,000 (₹1.4 to 2.8 crore).
- **Purpose:** Last sold in 1997 for charity, the dress will be displayed in London before touring Hong Kong and Geneva.

**Important points**
- Last sold in 1997 for charity, the dress will be displayed in London before touring Hong Kong and Geneva.

#### Drones light up NYC harbour to recreate Twin Towers on 25th anniversary of 9/11

**In short:** The skies above New York Harbour became a moving tribute on Wednesday ahead of the 25th anniversary of September 11, in an artwork titled “2,977 Lights Over New York Harbor.” According to AP, 2,977 synchronised drone lights memorialised every life lost in the 9/11 attacks, and at one point the lights joined to recreate the Twin Towers at full architectural scale as they stood before the 2001 attacks.
