### Sports

#### Hockey India strips Director General Srivastava of all powers

**In short:** Hockey India Director General Cdr. R.K.

Hockey India Director General Cdr. R.K. Srivastava was divested of all authority and responsibilities and served notice to explain repeated instances of non-compliance with presidential directives, including travelling to recent World Cups in the Netherlands and Belgium despite explicit directions from president Dilip Tirkey against it. The legal notice mentions arbitrary decisions on changing the national team's jersey without prior consultation and non-availability of POSH committee proceedings among other charges, with Srivastava asked to reply within four weeks with documentary evidence.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Hockey India strips Director General Srivastava of all powers
- **Told:** Hockey India Director General Cdr.
- **Purpose:** The legal notice mentions arbitrary decisions on changing the national team's jersey without prior consultation and non-availability of POSH committee proceedings among other charges, with Srivastava asked to reply within four weeks with documentary evidence.

_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.11; The Hindu Mangaluru p.15; Times of India Bengaluru p.20_

#### WUSHU PLAYER ALLEGES HARASSMENT AFTER GAMES AXE, FEDERATION CITES ACL TEAR

**In short:** Wushu player Divyanshi Choudhary has alleged she was dropped from India’s Asian Games squad to accommodate Roshibina Devi Naorem and accused the Wushu Association of India of physical and mental harassment, while the federation says her exclusion followed an MRI showing a high-grade ACL tear with large effusion. Divyanshi, who beat Roshibina in 60kg sanda selection trials, claimed mistreatment since June and threatened suicide in a video recorded on the NIS Patiala terrace; she attended the IOA send-off on September 8.

Top seed Tanvi Sharma lost 19-21, 20-22 to unseeded Sarunrak Vitidsarn of Thailand, and HS Prannoy fell 13-21, 8-21 to top seed Jia Heng Teh of Singapore in the men’s singles semi-final.

#### Red-hot Shelton downs Tiafoe

**In short:** Eighth seed Ben Shelton beat compatriot Frances Tiafoe 4-6, 6-3, 6-3, 7-5 with 20 aces at the US Open on Friday to reach his first Grand Slam final against Alexander Zverev, who defeated Karen Khachanov 6-3, 7-6 (9/7), 7-6 (8/6). Shelton, who eliminated defending champion Carlos Alcaraz in the quarterfinals, dedicated the win to his late grandmother and could become the first American men’s major champion since Andy Roddick in 2003.

#### MAAYA & ANTONINA LOSE TO BIRTHDAY GIRLS IN SEMIFINALS

**In short:** India’s Maaya Rajeshwaran Revathi and Ukraine’s Antonina Sushkova lost to American twins Kristina and Annika Penickova 6-7 (3), 2-6 in the US Open girls’ doubles semi-finals in New York. The Indo-Ukrainian pair rallied from 2-5 to 5-5 and served for the opening set before the Penickovas forced a tie-break they won from 5-1, then broke in the first and fifth games of the second set.

#### Duplantis and Ingebrigtsen steal the show on first day

**In short:** Scandinavian stars Armand Duplantis and Jakob Ingebrigtsen shone on the opening day of the inaugural Ultimate Championship in Budapest, each walking away with $150,000 for winning the men's pole vault and 5,000m respectively at the $10 million event spanning 28 disciplines. Duplantis cleared 6.20 metres ahead of Greek rival Emmanouil Karalis while Norway's Ingebrigtsen clocked 12:59.24, and World record holders Masai Russell and Ja'Kobe Tharp won the short hurdles races.

#### Razaullah's six-hitting debut caps Pak fightback

**In short:** Debutant No 9 batter Razaullah Khan's six-hitting helped Pakistan reach 449 on day three at Edgbaston for a 129-run lead, forcing a fourth day after the team had been expected to fold quickly against England's pace attack. England still needed 130 to complete a 3-0 series sweep.

**In short:** Teenager Sarthak Chavan shrugged off jet lag and a last-row start to win the premier Super Sport 301-400cc Open race in the second round of the MRF Indian National Racing Challenge 2W at the Madras International Circuit on Saturday, taking advantage of leader Rahil Pillarisetty's retirement. #### READY FOR THE BIG APPLE BITE

**In short:** Battle-hardened top seed Alexander Zverev beat Karen Khachanov 6-3, 7-6 (7), 7-6 (6) to reach his sixth Grand Slam final and third of 2026, and will face American Ben Shelton in Sunday’s US Open title match after Shelton defeated Frances Tiafoe 4-6, 6-3, 6-3, 7-5. Zverev, who broke a major drought at Roland Garros in June, hopes to add the US Open crown to his first Slam title and says he is ready for a sold-out Arthur Ashe Stadium crowd backing the home player.

- **Told:** Battle-hardened top seed Alexander Zverev beat Karen Khachanov 6-3, 7-6 (7), 7-6 (6) to reach his sixth Grand Slam final and third of 2026, and will face American Ben Shelton in Sunday’s US Open title match after Shelton defeated Frances Tiafoe 4-6, 6-3, 6-3, 7-5.
- **Purpose:** Zverev, who broke a major drought at Roland Garros in June, hopes to add the US Open crown to his first Slam title and says he is ready for a sold-out Arthur Ashe Stadium crowd backing the home player.

**Important points**

#### The First Black Man To Play In The US Open Final Since 1972 Is Not Overwhelmed By The Occasion

**In short:** Ben Shelton will become the first Black man to contest a US Open final since 1972 while chasing the first Queens title by an American man since Andy Roddick in 2003 and the first by a Black man since Arthur Ashe in 1968, yet the 23-year-old says he wants to cherish the moment without letting it overwhelm his tennis. Shelton, coached by his father Bryan Shelton, says he is a different player with more tools and is the most confident he has ever been despite trailing Alexander Zverev 5-0 in their head-to-head.

Ben Shelton will become the first Black man to contest a US Open final since 1972 while chasing the first Queens title by an American man since Andy Roddick in 2003 and the first by a Black man since Arthur Ashe in 1968, yet the 23-year-old says he wants to cherish the moment without letting it overwhelm his tennis. Shelton, coached by his father Bryan Shelton, says he is a different player with more tools and is the most confident he has ever been despite trailing Alexander Zverev 5-0 in their head-to-head.

#### 'High-flyer' Sehrawat ready to soar again

**In short:** Kabaddi star Pawan Sehrawat, nicknamed ‘High-flyer’ for his lion jumps and raiding, remains a vital cog as India chase another Asian Games gold after he captained the team to victory over Iran at Hangzhou 2022. Now deputy to captain Sunil Kumar, the 30-year-old from Bawana says the hunger to hear the national anthem on the podium is present in every player, and that India’s eight-to-nine-month preparation includes a video analyst in an “advanced approach.”

#### GRAPPLING WITH LEGACY

**In short:** From Manila 1954 to Hangzhou 2022, Indian wrestlers built a proud Asian Games record of 65 medals since 1951, but the 2022 edition yielded six medals with no gold, leaving the 2026 Aichi-Nagoya campaign as a chance to close unfinished business. An 18-strong squad chosen after May 30–31 trials was pruned to 15 after Deepanshu, Mukul Dahiya and Deepak tested positive for doping, though expectations remain high across men’s freestyle, women’s freestyle and Greco-Roman categories.

#### ASIAD-BOUND NEPAL BOXERS GET SAI SUPPORT TO TRAIN IN GUWAHATI

**In short:** The Sports Authority of India is hosting Nepal’s seven-member boxing team and two coaches at its Guwahati National Centre of Excellence since August 10 for joint training and sparring with Indian boxers ahead of the Asian Games. The camp, which continues till September 15 before the squad heads to Japan where competition starts September 19, has continued despite flash floods in Nepal, with SAI executive director DK Mittal calling it valuable exposure for Nepalese boxers.

#### Liverpool and Chelsea held

**In short:** Liverpool drew 0-0 at home to Fulham and Chelsea needed Joao Pedro’s 66th-minute equaliser to salvage a 2-2 draw at home to Hull City on Saturday, as Aston Villa lost 2-1 to Nottingham Forest, Ipswich beat 10-man Crystal Palace 3-2, and Bournemouth drew 2-2 with Brentford. Liverpool have now drawn three of their opening four Premier League games under Andoni Iraola, while Chelsea’s Morgan Rogers put them ahead in the seventh minute before Mohamed Belloumi struck twice for Hull before halftime.

#### Magnus loses but Pipers in final

**In short:** Grandmaster Alireza Firouzja beat world No. 1 Magnus Carlsen with the black pieces in 49 moves at the Global Chess League in Bengaluru on Saturday, handing Carlsen his first defeat this season and powering Triveni Kings to an 8-6 win over Alpine SG Pipers.

Grandmaster Alireza Firouzja beat world No. 1 Magnus Carlsen with the black pieces in 49 moves at the Global Chess League in Bengaluru on Saturday, handing Carlsen his first defeat this season and powering Triveni Kings to an 8-6 win over Alpine SG Pipers. With Carlsen losing on the icon board and Divya Deshmukh and Koneru Humpy drawing, defending champions Pipers still reached Sunday’s two-set final against Ganges Grandmasters after American Gambits lost 6-11 to Mumba Masters despite Javokhir Sindarov beating Maxime Vachier-Lagrave.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Magnus loses but Pipers in final
- **Told:** Grandmaster Alireza Firouzja beat world No.
- **Purpose:** With Carlsen losing on the icon board and Divya Deshmukh and Koneru Humpy drawing, defending champions Pipers still reached Sunday’s two-set final against Ganges Grandmasters after American Gambits lost 6-11 to Mumba Masters despite Javokhir Sindarov beating Maxime Vachier-Lagrave.

**Important points**
- With Carlsen losing on the icon board and Divya Deshmukh and Koneru Humpy drawing, defending champions Pipers still reached Sunday’s two-set final against Ganges Grandmasters after American Gambits lost 6-11 to Mumba Masters despite Javokhir Sindarov beating Maxime Vachier-Lagrave.
_Sources: Times of India Bengaluru p.20_

### Lifestyle

#### Mind in your hands

**In short:** Across urban India, exhausted professionals are turning to pottery, painting and other hands-on workshops for stress relief, with Callipottery founder Pradip and Bengaluru potter Apurva Chavan citing clay’s ego-dismantling effect and a 2026 Frontiers in Human Neuroscience scoping review linking art-making to lower cortisol — including a Drexel trial cutting average cortisol from ~17.9 to ~14.8 nmol/L in 45 minutes. Therapists and studio founders in Bengaluru, Hyderabad, Bhopal and Gurugram report reduced anxiety, new friendships, and dismantled perfectionism, while Bangalore Theatre Company’s Arjun Kabbina notes creative activity can act as a stressbuster without replacing clinical treatment.

#### The case for making things

**In short:** A sidebar notes that systematic reviews of craft and handmade activities — including a 2025 review of 19 craft-intervention studies and a 2024 review of 181 handmade-activity studies — associate hands-on making with better mood, lower stress, greater self-esteem, and stronger purpose. Working with clay, knitting or learning instruments demands attention and yields visible progress, challenging the brain with unfamiliar tasks in a world of passive digital consumption.

#### Politics of belonging

**In short:** Rahul Singh reviews Aruni Kashyap’s *How To Date A Fanatic* (Fourth Estate, pp 368, Rs 599), a novel expanding his 2020 Boston Review story about Delhi University assistant professor Rohit navigating queer desire, Assam roots, and a post-2014 authoritarian shift across Delhi, Assam and Haryana’s private-university belt. Singh praises Kashyap’s poetic political portrait of urban-rural porosity, reservation politics, and claustrophobic luxury amid campus change, and calls Minti the character who most haunts the reader after the book ends.

#### Hunt for selfhood amid daily chaos

**In short:** Aleksandra Bogdanova reviews Herta Müller’s *Traveling on One Leg* (Aleph, pp 154, Rs 599), a semi-autobiographical novel following Romanian-German Irene’s exile, loneliness, and failed romances after leaving Nicolae Ceausescu’s dictatorship for Germany. Bogdanova highlights Müller’s non-linear style, recurring water-mirror-window imagery, and Irene’s messy search for identity without marriage, career or neat resolution.

#### A comic lens to perceive the world

**In short:** Anjali Chauhan reviews *The Bare Bones Book of Humour* edited by Ankit Raj Ojha (Bare Bones Pubn, Rs 199), an anthology of 24 writers from eight countries arguing humour exposes vanity, power and everyday absurdity rather than offering escape. Chauhan singles out Merlin Flower’s feminist satire *Robes and Roles* and Aparna Kalra’s *Protection*, set against Uttarakhand’s Uniform Civil Code live-in registration rules, as politically incisive examples of comedy as dissent.

#### September is a month of thresholds: these three novels turn that transitional mood into stories of reunion, warmth and longing.

**In short:** Speed Reads surveys three September-set novels: Rosamunde Pilcher’s *September* reuniting MacNichol family and friends in the Scottish Highlands; Carissa Orlando’s haunted-house tale *The September House*; and Donna Tartt’s 1992 campus classic *The Secret History*, whose New England autumn atmosphere frames a privileged student group’s unraveling.

#### Read of the Week

**In short:** The column highlights Francis Fukuyama’s political memoir tracing his break from neoconservative mentors after the 2003 Iraq invasion and 2008 financial crash, alongside his father’s civil rights work and CIA contract research in the 1980s. It frames the book as an urgent study of how the West can recover democracy amid shifts from Bush through Obama to Trump and Russia’s Ukraine invasion.

#### A city of contrasts

**In short:** Pallabi Dey Purkayastha’s 48-hour Bhopal guide covers Upper Lake at dawn, Qudsia Begum’s 1820 Gohar Mahal, 1830 Shaukat Mahal, Shah Jahan Begum’s Taj-ul-Masajid, Bharat Bhavan’s performing arts, Chowk Bazaar street food and zardosi shopping, plus day-two trips to Sanchi Stupa, Indira Gandhi Rashtriya Manav Sangrahalaya’s tribal architecture displays, Van Vihar National Park, and a sunset boat ride before departing from Raja Bhoj Airport.

#### A kadubu by any other name…

**In short:** Origin Story traces the sweet dumpling known as kadubu, kudumu, kozhukatta, modak and, in Goa, Mangaluru and coastal Karnataka, fried neureo or nevri, from Sanskrit *moda-ka* (“that which delights”) through Vedic honey fillings, Buddhist-period jaggery, Someshwara III’s *Manasollasa*, and global parallels such as pierogi and tangyuan. Food historian K T Achaya links modaka to medical “sweet uncooked pills” in Charaka and Sushrutha, while Barbara Gallani notes independent dumpling traditions spread through travel and trade.

#### Culinary trail across time zones

**In short:** Ruth Dsouza Prabhu reports that beyond Ajanta-Ellora at Chhatrapati Sambhajinagar, food now shapes travel through Dhyaana Farms’ curated farm meals, Chef Mohib Farooqui’s eight-seater Accentuate Food Lab tasting menus of nine to thirteen themed courses, street stops such as Hira Pao Bhaji and Mathura Wala, paan rituals, Jama Khan Mandi and Mustafa Biryani, and local staples including naan qalia, Aurangabad kantakki, tikiya pao, samosa rice and imarti.

#### Truth about remission

**In short:** Dr R Shrikanth, a Hubli hemato-oncologist, explains that leukaemia “complete remission” means no detectable disease on standard tests, not guaranteed eradication of all malignant cells, with measurable residual disease (MRD) offering earlier relapse signals. He cites 2025 AML studies showing 49.7% five-year relapse among transplant patients with detectable leukaemia stem cells versus 8.5% without, and notes newer options including targeted therapy, immunotherapy and pre-emptive azacitidine when MRD turns positive.

#### Multivitamins: Yes or no?

**In short:** Mythbuster examines mixed evidence on daily multivitamins, citing a 2026 Columbia University *Nature Medicine* study finding a minor biological-ageing slowdown alongside a 2024 JAMA Network Open study of 4,00,000 US adults over two decades showing no mortality benefit. It advises healthy adults to prioritise balanced food and sun exposure, follow doctor-prescribed supplementation for deficiencies, and avoid overdosing rather than taking pills “on autopilot.”

#### How lapis lazuli coloured the world

**In short:** Shrey Maurya’s Discover Indian Art column traces lapis lazuli from Afghanistan’s Sar-e-Sang mines — nearly the world’s sole source until around 1950 — along Silk Road trade routes into Indus Valley beadwork from 2600 BCE, Buddhist cave pigments, Persian and Indian manuscript ateliers, and European ultramarine used by Leonardo da Vinci, Vermeer and others until 19th-century synthetics. Archaeological and museum evidence shows the stone prized for colour and supposed spiritual protection across Egypt, Nepal and beyond.

Archaeological and museum evidence shows the stone prized for colour and supposed spiritual protection across Egypt, Nepal and beyond.

#### CRACKER of a TRACKER

**In short:** Triumph's Tracker 400, India's first production street-tracker inspired by flat-track racing, pairs a Thruxton-tuned 349-cc single with a 13-litre tank, 805-mm seat, quick-shifter and firm suspension suited to tarmac rather than off-road use. A test ride on Pune's outskirts found strong braking, predictable dual-purpose tyre grip even in rain, and agile cornering from the shortest wheelbase in Triumph's 400 range.

#### UNDERSTANDING EARLY FACIAL Wrinkles: Causes and prevention strategies

**In short:** Premature facial wrinkles are driven largely by photoaging from UV exposure accounting for up to 80% of visible aging, plus dehydration, repetitive expressions, glycation from refined sugar, smoking, pollution, stress and poor sleep. Prevention includes daily SPF 30+ sunscreen, retinoids, vitamin C, barrier-repair moisturisers, hydration, silk pillowcases, anti-inflammatory diet and 7-9 hours of sleep, with dermatologist consultation advised for persistent concerns.

#### A PAINLESS LUMP IS NOT ALWAYS HARMLESS Doctors decode the silent signs of sarcoma

**In short:** Doctors warn that painless lumps that keep growing, feel firm or fixed, exceed about 5 cm or recur after removal need evaluation because they may signal sarcoma in muscle, fat, nerves or bone. Diagnosis involves examination, ultrasound or MRI and core-needle biopsy, with surgery often central and radiation, chemotherapy or immunotherapy considered at specialist centres.

**In short:** Raw vegetables preserve heat-sensitive vitamin C, folate and enzymes in broccoli, bell peppers, beets, garlic, onions and leafy greens better than cooking. #### Rooted memory museum

**In short:** Pema's Blossoms, a 1,600-sq-ft Pelling apartment designed by makeomadesigns for air hostess Mrs Pema Bhutia, is conceived as a rooted memory museum centred on the lotus motif from her name. The kitchen island serves as the welcoming heart aligned with Sikkimese hospitality, while a Karma Gyalchup Bhutia lotus mural and a divan living archive display travel keepsakes and family photographs.

#### Celine Dion poised for emotional comeback

**In short:** Celine Dion was set to make her long-awaited return to the stage on Saturday in Paris after years battling a rare neurological disorder, performing her first full concert in six years. Hours before the show, the 58-year-old French Canadian singer greeted admirers outside her luxury hotel near the Arc de Triomphe as fan anticipation reached fever pitch.

#### Amid climate change, more Brits ditch tea for ice cream

**In short:** Britain's tea consumption is wilting as climate change drives record heat, with Associated British Foods reporting dampened demand for hot beverages at Twinings and Ovaltine as temperatures rose as high as 38°C during western Europe's hottest summer on record, including 40 days above 30°C in the UK. Retailers from Dunelm to RevolutionRace cited heat-driven high-street declines, while Currys benefited from cooling-appliance sales and Magnum Ice Cream reported higher-than-expected growth in July.

#### A timeless tale, retold futuristically

**In short:** Daffodils Foundation for Learning marked its decennial Annual Day, Inspirit 3, with the theme "Modern Chronicles of Vikram and Betaal," featuring students of classes 6 to 10 in drama, dance, music and visual effects that reimagined Betaal as a Spiritoid adviser to King Vikramaditya. A milestones video by founding teachers highlighted a decade of growth, and the presentation conveyed that technology may assist but cannot replace human beings.

_Sources: Times of India Bengaluru p.5_

#### All rice! An old drinking grain finds new spirit

**In short:** Indian distillers are turning heirloom rice—from Handia and Judima traditions to ukdo, chak hao, ambemohar and Basmati—into beer, whisky, gin and vodka. Diageo’s Epitome Reserve (2021) was a 100% rice whisky; Mysuru’s Huli now makes “rice malt” whisky from malted local paddy, producing about 70,000 bottles a year after four years of R&D.

Indian distillers are turning heirloom rice—from Handia and Judima traditions to ukdo, chak hao, ambemohar and Basmati—into beer, whisky, gin and vodka. Diageo’s Epitome Reserve (2021) was a 100% rice whisky; Mysuru’s Huli now makes “rice malt” whisky from malted local paddy, producing about 70,000 bottles a year after four years of R&D. Goa Brewing Company builds 95% of its beer on rice, sourcing from ~180 farming families at 25% above MSP; last year unseasonal rain wiped out nearly 90% of crop in some Goa talukas. Smoke Lab’s Basmati-based vodka has sold over 150,000 cases in the US; Jin Jiji gin from Kumaon rice is poured at top US bars.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** All rice! An old drinking grain finds new spirit
- **Told:** Indian distillers are turning heirloom rice—from Handia and Judima traditions to ukdo, chak hao, ambemohar and Basmati—into beer, whisky, gin and vodka.
- **Purpose:** Smoke Lab’s Basmati-based vodka has sold over 150,000 cases in the US; Jin Jiji gin from Kumaon rice is poured at top US bars.

#### Finding our way home may be the final frontier

**In short:** On Star Trek’s 60th anniversary, Jai Arjun Singh recalls discovering the series’ colour via a US-returned aunt’s videocassette after watching it in black-and-white on Doordarshan in the mid-1980s, and how Kirk, Spock and the Enterprise crew shaped a generation of Indian viewers. He notes later iterations including Next Generation’s gender-neutral “where no one has gone before,” and cites astronauts Swati Mohan and Sirisha Bandla crediting Star Trek for their space careers.

On Star Trek’s 60th anniversary, Jai Arjun Singh recalls discovering the series’ colour via a US-returned aunt’s videocassette after watching it in black-and-white on Doordarshan in the mid-1980s, and how Kirk, Spock and the Enterprise crew shaped a generation of Indian viewers. He notes later iterations including Next Generation’s gender-neutral “where no one has gone before,” and cites astronauts Swati Mohan and Sirisha Bandla crediting Star Trek for their space careers. William Shatner, after visiting space at age 90, wrote that Earth “is, and will remain, our only home”—tempering the adventure ethos with a call to preserve the planet.

#### Meet the bots steeling the spotlight!

**In short:** Humanoid robots imported from China and programmed in India are moonlighting as wedding dancers, store-inauguration chief guests and reality-show contestants—Jeevan at a Ujjain sandwich shop, Robo Maveli at a Kochi mall Onam event, and Maya on India’s Got Latent. China shipped 40,000+ humanoids in H1 2026 (97% of global shipments); Unitree says average humanoid prices fell 72% between 2023 and 2025.

#### Walls take a curve

**In short:** Curved walls are moving from furniture accents into luxury interior architecture, with designers using aluminium framing, flexi-ply and MDF finished in paint or stone to soften straight lines in living rooms and passageways. Experts say curves work best to define zones in open plans or guide movement in corridors, pair well with sculptural lighting and statement art rather than competing decor, and demand skilled craftsmanship—though overly tight curves or too many rounded elements can overwhelm a space.

#### FORGET CELEBRITIES, ASK THE ATHLETE

**In short:** A Business of Fashion Insights report on fashion and sports e-commerce finds athletes are the most trusted public figures for product recommendations, with almost a third of consumers likely to act on their advice compared with 17% for celebrities and macro-influencers and 19% for micro-influencers. The survey suggests influence is shifting from scale to specificity, even as 73% of consumers say friends and family remain the strongest purchase drivers.

#### COST PER WEAR: FASHION'S NEW ECONOMIC MODEL

**In short:** Cost per wear divides a garment’s price by expected wears, and research by the University of Bath and Cambridge Judge Business School published in Psychology & Marketing found shoppers shown CPW figures were more likely to choose pricier, higher-quality items over cheaper alternatives. Applied like supermarket unit pricing, a CPW label could make a ₹15,000 long-lasting coat look different from a ₹3,000 piece needing frequent replacement, signalling a shift from upfront price to value per wear that luxury brands emphasizing craftsmanship may benefit from most.

#### Cluttercore is bringing soul back to homes

**In short:** After years of minimal interiors, cluttercore is celebrating meaningful accumulation—layering books, art, ceramics, photographs and vintage finds to create personal, lived-in spaces rather than random clutter. Designers quoted in The Spruce and House Beautiful say curated collections with breathing space, grouped objects and varied heights add warmth and character, with Pinterest’s 2026 Hobbies Trend Report highlighting “Curated Clutter” as homeowners seek tactile, expressive rooms built around micro-collections that tell their story.

#### How to: Add millet to your daily meals

**In short:** Mix cooked millets 50/50 with rice for grain bowls, or swap a quarter of regular flour with ragi or jowar when baking. Keep foxtail millet to replace rice and ragi flour for breakfast; use whole sorghum or pearl millet for salads and soups.

Mix cooked millets 50/50 with rice for grain bowls, or swap a quarter of regular flour with ragi or jowar when baking. Keep foxtail millet to replace rice and ragi flour for breakfast; use whole sorghum or pearl millet for salads and soups. Soak grains for 4 to 6 hours before cooking, and dry-roast raw millet for 3 minutes for a nutty flavour. Build habits with ragi porridge at breakfast, millet in lunch salads, khichdi for dinner, and puffed millets as a snack.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** How to: Add millet to your daily meals
- **Told:** Mix cooked millets 50/50 with rice for grain bowls, or swap a quarter of regular flour with ragi or jowar when baking.
- **Purpose:** Build habits with ragi porridge at breakfast, millet in lunch salads, khichdi for dinner, and puffed millets as a snack.

**Important points**
- Soak grains for 4 to 6 hours before cooking, and dry-roast raw millet for 3 minutes for a nutty flavour.
- Build habits with ragi porridge at breakfast, millet in lunch salads, khichdi for dinner, and puffed millets as a snack.
_Sources: Times of India Bengaluru p.23_

### Other

#### Wakey, wakey

**In short:** The human body's circadian rhythm, governed by the suprachiasmatic nucleus in the brain, prepares the body for wakefulness in the hours before a habitual waking time through rising stress hormone levels and increasing body temperature. Waking a few minutes before an alarm can reflect this learned cycle rather than pure coincidence or foresight, as people tend to remember uncanny successes like waking seconds before the alarm and forget mornings when they woke much earlier for no apparent reason.

#### AMEESHA PATEL’S PARENTS’ MUMBAI HOME ROBBED `5 LAKH JEWELLERY STOLEN

**In short:** Ameesha Patel’s parents’ Churchgate home in Mumbai was allegedly burgled, with gold and diamond jewellery worth around ₹5 lakh reported missing. Police have arrested a 40-year-old domestic help who had worked at Ameesha’s mother Asha Patel’s residence for nearly 1.5 years, and the court remanded her to police custody until September 15.

#### I'm glad he accepted the invitation, says Ajith on Vijay flagging off Le Mans race

**In short:** Actor-racer Ajith Kumar welcomed Tamil Nadu Chief Minister C Joseph Vijay flagging off Round 5 of the 2026 Michelin Le Mans Cup at Silverstone on September 12, thanking him for visiting despite a busy schedule after 34 years as colleagues in the film industry. Ajith said he was glad Vijay accepted the invitation to assess what it would take for Chennai to host events like the Le Mans series, noting the state government’s broader push to promote sports beyond motorsport.

#### I'm in a zone now where I want to be more present in every moment: Saif

**In short:** Saif Ali Khan, speaking as Haiwaan plays in theatres and a Bombay Times Lounge episode goes live, said he wants to balance life well, do good work and be more present in every moment rather than let stardom distract from what matters. He reflected on staying grounded, valuing criticism and creating screen moments that are remembered, adding that a handful of good films can make a career worthwhile even if not every project registers as hoped.

#### Women lose screen space, but gain Emmy spotlight

**In short:** Female representation declined across the 2025–26 US television season even as women creators punched above their weight among Emmy nominees, a Center for the Study of Women in Television and Film at San Diego State University study found. Female creators on streaming fell from 36% to 24%, major female characters dropped to 44% of speaking roles, yet women accounted for 31% of nominated comedy and drama shows despite making up only about 22% of creators, and female directors received 42% of Outstanding Director nominations in those categories despite comprising 20% of directors.

Female representation declined across the 2025–26 US television season even as women creators punched above their weight among Emmy nominees, a Center for the Study of Women in Television and Film at San Diego State University study found. Female creators on streaming fell from 36% to 24%, major female characters dropped to 44% of speaking roles, yet women accounted for 31% of nominated comedy and drama shows despite making up only about 22% of creators, and female directors received 42% of Outstanding Director nominations in those categories despite comprising 20% of directors.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Women lose screen space, but gain Emmy spotlight
- **Told:** Female representation declined across the 2025–26 US television season even as women creators punched above their weight among Emmy nominees, a Center for the Study of Women in Television and Film at San Diego State University study found.
- **Purpose:** Female creators on streaming fell from 36% to 24%, major female characters dropped to 44% of speaking roles, yet women accounted for 31% of nominated comedy and drama shows despite making up only about 22% of creators, and female directors received 42% of Outstanding Director nominations in those categories despite comprising 20% of directors.

**Important points**
- Female representation declined across the 2025–26 US television season even as women creators punched above their weight among Emmy nominees, a Center for the Study of Women in Television and Film at San Diego State University study found. Female creators on streaming fell from 36% to 24%, major female characters dropped to 44% of speaking roles, yet women accounted for 31% of nominated comedy and drama shows despite making up only about 22% of creators, and female directors received 42% of Outstanding Director nominations in those categories despite comprising 20% of directors.
_Sources: Times of India Bengaluru p.21_

#### 1. BRICS leaders seek stronger cooperation over AI safety

**In short:** BRICS leaders on Saturday called for wider access to AI resources and stronger international cooperation to ensure the technology is safe, secure, inclusive and reliable, committing to implement the grouping's statement on "Global Governance of artificial intelligence". The New Delhi Declaration 2026 laid out a broad technology agenda spanning AI, cybersecurity, digital public infrastructure, telecom, submarine networks, quantum technologies, startups and advanced manufacturing, and pushed for concerted efforts to address digital security threats, foster AI for science and innovation, develop trustworthy AI technologies and improve energy efficiency of AI systems.

#### 2. Being a woman, living in a world of fear

**In short:** Globally one in three women has experienced physical or sexual violence per UN estimates, and India reported over 4 lakh crimes against women in 2024—over 1,200 daily per NCRB—as deep-rooted patriarchy and India’s rank of 131 of 148 on the Global Gender Gap Index 2025 compound unsafe public transport, workplaces and digital spaces. A 2025 LinkedIn survey found about 70% of Indian corporate women experienced workplace harassment, with many not reporting for fear of retaliation and willing to forgo 19% of wages for safer workplaces; cybercrimes against women rose 239% between 2017 and 2022 per a Joint Parliamentary Committee report, while legal safeguards including BNS sections and the POSH Act face gaps in street-harassment coverage, police sensitisation, digital-law enforcement and low conviction rates that discourage complaints.

#### 3. PM urges BRICS leaders to draw up 10 global governance reform proposals

**In short:** Prime Minister Narendra Modi on Saturday made a strong pitch for global reform, highlighting the imbalance between decision-makers and those impacted by their decisions, and called for BRICS to transition from "rule-takers to rule-shapers". He proposed that leaders at the 18th BRICS Summit arrive at 10 proposals for global governance reform to develop into a BRICS Reform Road map, a BRICS Continuity and Implementation Mechanism for the Troika to follow up on initiatives, and a secure digital repository tracking nodal points, deadlines and implementation status.

Prime Minister Narendra Modi on Saturday made a strong pitch for global reform, highlighting the imbalance between decision-makers and those impacted by their decisions, and called for BRICS to transition from "rule-takers to rule-shapers". He proposed that leaders at the 18th BRICS Summit arrive at 10 proposals for global governance reform to develop into a BRICS Reform Road map, a BRICS Continuity and Implementation Mechanism for the Troika to follow up on initiatives, and a secure digital repository tracking nodal points, deadlines and implementation status. Mr. Modi stressed reform of the UN Security Council with text-based negotiations linked to a definite timeline, voting power and leadership positions in financial institutions reflecting today's economic realities, a Seafarers' Emergency Support Network, and transforming the Global South from rule-takers into rule-shapers in AI, cyberspace, outer space and biotechnology.

**What this is**

#### 4. Going 1980s? Police, experts red-flag retro avatar trend

**In short:** Bengaluru police and cybersecurity experts have warned citizens against uploading high-quality selfies to unregulated third-party apps for Instagram's 1980s AI photo trend, saying such apps can harvest biometric data for facial recognition, deepfakes, identity theft and scams. DCP (crime) Imam Raja Kasim said miscreants use AI-generated images of others as display pictures to impersonate reputed persons and lure victims into sharing personal or financial data, sometimes obtaining banking credentials; police have spread warnings through posters and social media and advised using verified platforms, stripping EXIF metadata and turning off AI model training.

Senior IT expert and Kannada and Tulu Wikipedia admin U.B.

At a panel on 'Tulu Wikipedia in the age of AI' during the 10th anniversary celebrations in Mangaluru on Saturday, Wikimedians said artificial intelligence is not a threat to Wikipedia if content is thoroughly verified, filtered, edited and used. Senior IT expert and Kannada and Tulu Wikipedia admin U.B. Pavanaja said most Wikimedians in England oppose AI-generated content and have a policy against it, while Karavali Wikimedians User Group treasurer Kishore Kumar Rai Sheni warned that direct use of AI content is dangerous and undermines creative writing. Tamil Wikipedia admin Neechal Karan noted ancient Tamil literature and Tamil Nadu's state song reference Tulu Nadu, Kadri Manjunatha temple and Mangaladevi temple, and speakers urged more Tulu fiction online and youth engagement in writing for Tulu Wikipedia.

### More technology

#### AI can do the work, can students do the thinking?

**In short:** Agentic AI reshapes learning and hiring by taking a goal, breaking it into steps, choosing tools, executing and checking results, unlike ordinary chatbots that only answer questions. Nearly half of ChatGPT’s Indian users are under 24 and use it for studying, while a PNAS randomised trial found school students with unrestricted GPT-4 access scored 48% better on practice problems but 17% worse on unaided exams when the tool was withdrawn.

Agentic AI reshapes learning and hiring by taking a goal, breaking it into steps, choosing tools, executing and checking results, unlike ordinary chatbots that only answer questions. Nearly half of ChatGPT’s Indian users are under 24 and use it for studying, while a PNAS randomised trial found school students with unrestricted GPT-4 access scored 48% better on practice problems but 17% worse on unaided exams when the tool was withdrawn. The piece warns of metacognitive offloading, false confidence from flawless AI output, and a labour-market mismatch as Indian IT fresher hiring fell from about 6 lakh in FY 2022 to about 1.2 lakh in FY 2025 even as employability rates rose.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** AI can do the work, can students do the thinking?
- **Told:** Agentic AI reshapes learning and hiring by taking a goal, breaking it into steps, choosing tools, executing and checking results, unlike ordinary chatbots that only answer questions.
- **Purpose:** The piece warns of metacognitive offloading, false confidence from flawless AI output, and a labour-market mismatch as Indian IT fresher hiring fell from about 6 lakh in FY 2022 to about 1.2 lakh in FY 2025 even as employability rates rose.

**Important points**
- The piece warns of metacognitive offloading, false confidence from flawless AI output, and a labour-market mismatch as Indian IT fresher hiring fell from about 6 lakh in FY 2022 to about 1.2 lakh in FY 2025 even as employability rates rose.

#### Saudis shut key pipeline after attack, spark fears of further fuel price rise

**In short:** Saudi Arabia temporarily shut its 1,200km East-West pipeline as a precaution after a drone attack that both Baghdad and Riyadh said originated in Iraq, where Iranian-backed militias operate; Iraq dismissed a commander on Saturday after the strike. The pipeline has moved 4 million to 5 million barrels per day—4% to 5% of global supply—serving as the main West Asian oil export route while the Strait of Hormuz has been largely shut by war, and satellite images showed black smoke rising south of Medina.

#### Why is NCPCR investigating Meta, Instagram?

**In short:** Following a BBC investigation that found Instagram running paid advertisements promoting child sexual exploitation and abuse material in India, the National Commission for Protection of Child Rights summoned Meta officials for a hearing on September 9 and the Ministry of Electronics and Information Technology also summoned the company. Automatic moderation systems and AI detection cannot catch every violative submission, and the Tech Transparency Project reported that Meta sometimes classified removed CSEAM ads as adult sexual content and rejected 57% of reported CSEAM ads as not violating Advertising Standards.

Following a BBC investigation that found Instagram running paid advertisements promoting child sexual exploitation and abuse material in India, the National Commission for Protection of Child Rights summoned Meta officials for a hearing on September 9 and the Ministry of Electronics and Information Technology also summoned the company. Automatic moderation systems and AI detection cannot catch every violative submission, and the Tech Transparency Project reported that Meta sometimes classified removed CSEAM ads as adult sexual content and rejected 57% of reported CSEAM ads as not violating Advertising Standards. NCPCR, which has powers of a civil court under the Commissions for Protection of Child Rights Act, 2005, can demand explanations from private organisations on child safety issues and has previously issued notices to companies including Netflix and Mondelez.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Why is NCPCR investigating Meta, Instagram?
- **Told:** Following a BBC investigation that found Instagram running paid advertisements promoting child sexual exploitation and abuse material in India, the National Commission for Protection of Child Rights summoned Meta officials for a hearing on September 9 and the Ministry of Electronics and Information Technology also summoned the company.
- **Purpose:** NCPCR, which has powers of a civil court under the Commissions for Protection of Child Rights Act, 2005, can demand explanations from private organisations on child safety issues and has previously issued notices to companies including Netflix and Mondelez.

**Important points**
- NCPCR, which has powers of a civil court under the Commissions for Protection of Child Rights Act, 2005, can demand explanations from private organisations on child safety issues and has previously issued notices to companies including Netflix and Mondelez.
_Sources: The Hindu Mangaluru p.11_

#### Malaysia, Ethiopia seek stronger defence cooperation with India

**In short:** Prime Minister Narendra Modi met Malaysian and Ethiopian counterparts Anwar Ibrahim and Abiy Ahmed Ali on the sidelines of the Brics summit Saturday, with officials saying Malaysia and Ethiopia expressed keenness for stronger defence cooperation with India including joint personnel training and defence procurement. Ethiopia welcomed Indian mining companies, Malaysia agreed to opening a new Indian consulate, and opportunities in the semiconductor industry were discussed with Malaysia; Ibrahim conveyed Malaysia's concurrence to establishment of a consulate general of India in Kota Kinabalu, Sabah.

Prime Minister Narendra Modi met Malaysian and Ethiopian counterparts Anwar Ibrahim and Abiy Ahmed Ali on the sidelines of the Brics summit Saturday, with officials saying Malaysia and Ethiopia expressed keenness for stronger defence cooperation with India including joint personnel training and defence procurement. The meeting with Abiy Ahmed built on Modi's visit to Addis Ababa in Dec 2025, reviewing progress across economic cooperation, defence and security partnership and capacity building.

#### Anthropic says UAE-linked AI op targeted UN staff over Sudan

**In short:** AI firm Anthropic said it detected and disrupted a UAE-linked influence operation that targeted the Muslim Brotherhood and United Nations experts critical of Abu Dhabi's alleged role in Sudan's war, linking the campaign "with high confidence" to UAE government officials. Using Anthropic's Claude chatbot, the operation generated reports, advocacy materials, social media content and testimony on Sudan, compiled "counter-accountability dossiers" on UN special rapporteurs who criticised the UAE, and appropriated the identity of a Sudanese human rights organisation to draft testimony for named individuals.

#### Let outsiders evaluate AI safety: Anthropic chief

**In short:** Anthropic CEO Dario Amodei called for all frontier AI companies to give "ongoing, employee-like access" to outside evaluators to monitor safety practices, days after Anthropic blocked malicious uses of its models and following OpenAI's report of an AI system hacking another AI company in July. Amodei also urged democratic governments to coordinate with authoritarian ones so rivals do not accelerate when US firms intentionally pace development, as researcher resignations from Anthropic's safety team underscored industry tensions over responsible AI deployment.

#### Dario, Altman & Musk: AI devpt needs to slow down

**In short:** Anthropic CEO Dario Amodei said Saturday the AI industry should slow development so safety measures can catch up, warning that without pacing, AI could within six to 12 months lead a swarm capable of taking over the entire internet. OpenAI CEO Sam Altman backed the call on X, committing to independent evaluators with employee-like access, and Tesla boss Elon Musk posted "Dario is right," as Amodei proposed industry and government coordination including with authoritarian states.

#### Tolstoy's influence on Gandhi had a more profound impact than his books

**In short:** Daniil Tolstoy, Leo Tolstoy's great-grandson, and Gopalkrishna Gandhi, Mahatma Gandhi's grandson, have revisited the century-old correspondence between their ancestors in 'Truth and Love: The Gandhi-Tolstoy Letters', with Daniil telling Sneha Bhura that although the seven letters exchanged in 1909–10 are known in scholarly circles, the wider public is largely unaware of the close connection, and that Tolstoy's influence on Gandhi — drawn to the moral radical behind 'The Kingdom of God Is Within You' rather than the novelist of 'War and Peace' — had a more profound impact on the world than his literature. Daniil, an academic and organic farmer whose family fled Russia after the 1917 Revolution and who is now cut off from his farm there because of the war, argues that in an era of algorithmic control, ecological collapse and militarised conflict, their ideas remind us that genuine survival depends on human conscience choosing truth and non-violence over brute force.

Daniil Tolstoy, Leo Tolstoy's great-grandson, and Gopalkrishna Gandhi, Mahatma Gandhi's grandson, have revisited the century-old correspondence between their ancestors in 'Truth and Love: The Gandhi-Tolstoy Letters', with Daniil telling Sneha Bhura that although the seven letters exchanged in 1909–10 are known in scholarly circles, the wider public is largely unaware of the close connection, and that Tolstoy's influence on Gandhi — drawn to the moral radical behind 'The Kingdom of God Is Within You' rather than the novelist of 'War and Peace' — had a more profound impact on the world than his literature. #### Anthropic CEO urges AI companies to slow model development

**In short:** Anthropic CEO Dario Amodei called on AI companies to slow the rate at which they advance model capabilities amid mounting fears of misuse of artificial intelligence, outlining a three-step framework intended to pace development and create more time to manage its risks. Both Elon Musk of xAI and Sam Altman of OpenAI said they agree with Mr.

Anthropic CEO Dario Amodei called on AI companies to slow the rate at which they advance model capabilities amid mounting fears of misuse of artificial intelligence, outlining a three-step framework intended to pace development and create more time to manage its risks. Both Elon Musk of xAI and Sam Altman of OpenAI said they agree with Mr. Amodei, whose plan calls for independent reviewers inside leading AI companies, coordination among frontier AI firms to set safety standards and limit unchecked AI development, and international cooperation to manage AI risks.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Anthropic CEO urges AI companies to slow model development
- **Told:** Anthropic CEO Dario Amodei called on AI companies to slow the rate at which they advance model capabilities amid mounting fears of misuse of artificial intelligence, outlining a three-step framework intended to pace development and create more time to manage its risks.
- **Purpose:** Amodei, whose plan calls for independent reviewers inside leading AI companies, coordination among frontier AI firms to set safety standards and limit unchecked AI development, and international cooperation to manage AI risks.

#### Brics AI plan leaves question of jobs largely unanswered

**In short:** The BRICS New Delhi Declaration sets an ambitious plan to prepare people for an AI-driven economy but largely sidesteps what happens to workers whose jobs are lost or sharply changed by automation and AI. It frames AI as an opportunity for growth and sustainable development while stressing safety, security, inclusion and reliability, and commits members to forecasting skills needs by analysing labour-market trends in digital, green, care and other priority sectors—rather than spelling out protections when technology replaces existing work.

#### AI formalises Fermat's Last Theorem proof in 11 days

**In short:** Anthropic has used its Claude AI to formalise Fermat's Last Theorem using the Lean language, translating human arguments into code so software can verify every logical step from first principles without relying on human trust. While mathematicians had anticipated the translation would take five years of painstaking work, Claude achieved it in 11 days, generating over 13 million lines of code and verifying 29,500 intermediate theorems in a breakthrough for automated reasoning.

#### 1 in 3 new webpages now shows AI's fingerprints

**In short:** Since January 2023, when ChatGPT was released, over a third of all new webpages have either been edited by AI or written by it, according to a Pew Research random sample of 10,000 pages charting the share of webpages showing AI editing or authorship from Jan 2023 through Jul 2026, when the figure stood at about 35%.

## Opinion & Explainers

### The AI skill students are not being taught (Opinion)

**In short:** A student who cannot distinguish correct from merely plausible AI output has acquired no real capability as fresher IT hiring fell from about 6 lakh in FY 2022 to 1.2 lakh in FY 2025 while employability metrics rose, leaving employers wanting judgement employers universities may not build. Safe Agentic AI adoption requires UGC/AICTE accreditation policy on where tools may accompany versus replace learning, treating agentic systems as legal objects with DPDPA Section 9 constraints for minors, and sectoral education regulation as MeitY’s November 2025 India AI Governance Guidelines delegate rulemaking while computational thinking becomes mandatory for Class 3 in 2026–27 with barely 15% AI-literate teachers.

- A student who cannot distinguish correct from merely plausible AI output has acquired no real capability as fresher IT hiring fell from about 6 lakh in FY 2022 to 1.2 lakh in FY 2025 while employability metrics rose, leaving employers wanting judgement employers universities may not build.
- Safe Agentic AI adoption requires UGC/AICTE accreditation policy on where tools may accompany versus replace learning, treating agentic systems as legal objects with DPDPA Section 9 constraints for minors, and sectoral education regulation as MeitY’s November 2025 India AI Governance Guidelines delegate rulemaking while computational thinking becomes mandatory for Class 3 in 2026–27 with barely 15% AI-literate teachers.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.4_

### BRICS supplies 42% of India's imports, buys only 22% of its exports (Opinion)

**In short:** Ajay Srivastava writes India's BRICS goods trade rose from $203 billion in FY2021 to $417.5 billion in FY2026 as imports jumped 131.8% to $321.8 billion while exports grew 48.8% to $95.7 billion, tripling the deficit to $226.1 billion. China supplied 41% of BRICS imports, intra-BRICS trade remains China-centred at 4-5% of world trade, and India must seek better market access to avoid widening imbalance.

He cautions against anti-American de-dollarisation fantasies, noting China's GDP alone exceeds ten other BRICS members combined and multilateral summits should sober India about building strength rather than triumphalism.

### Germany’s far-right movement is no longer on the fringe (Opinion)

**In short:** Alternative for Germany won about 44% and 39 of 83 seats in Saxony-Anhalt state elections, more than doubling its prior support while Chancellor Friedrich Merz’s CDU fell to 17.2% and 15 seats with about 78% turnout—the AfD’s best result though three seats short of a majority. The outcome pressures Merz ahead of Berlin and Mecklenburg-Western Pomerania polls, emboldens Europe’s far right, and raises questions about sustaining the political firewall isolating the AfD as it leads national polls at about 28% with policies on migration, energy, Ukraine and Russia complicating European integration and ReArm Europe defence plans.

- Alternative for Germany won about 44% and 39 of 83 seats in Saxony-Anhalt state elections, more than doubling its prior support while Chancellor Friedrich Merz’s CDU fell to 17.2% and 15 seats with about 78% turnout—the AfD’s best result though three seats short of a majority.
- The outcome pressures Merz ahead of Berlin and Mecklenburg-Western Pomerania polls, emboldens Europe’s far right, and raises questions about sustaining the political firewall isolating the AfD as it leads national polls at about 28% with policies on migration, energy.
- Ukraine and Russia complicating European integration and ReArm Europe defence plans.

### Why India's schools should take note of New York's AI ban

**In short:** New York City Mayor Zohran Mamdani and Schools Chancellor Kamar H. Samuels unveiled sweeping curbs on AI use and screen time in the city's public schools on September 2, barring student-facing AI tools for kindergarten through eighth grade for a year and imposing age-calibrated screen time limits affecting over 600,000 students.

- New York City Mayor Zohran Mamdani and Schools Chancellor Kamar H.
- Samuels unveiled sweeping curbs on AI use and screen time in the city's public schools on September 2, barring student-facing AI tools for kindergarten through eighth grade for a year and imposing age-calibrated screen time limits affecting over 600,000 students.
- John Xavier argues India should pay close attention as ASER's 2024 survey found 82.2% of children aged 14-16 know how to operate a smartphone but only a little over half use it for anything educational, with India's device deficit in schools potentially a blessing if paired with age-tiered policies, evaluation mechanisms, and attention to smartphone use at home rather than waiting for a crisis.

### How Brics went from supporting actor to main-character energy (Opinion)

**In short:** Daulet Singh argues that a decade ago Brics was seen as a supporting actor in US-led order-building, but the US has become a spoiler through weaponisation of public goods, and Brics is now the only multilateral institution upholding interconnections between diverse economies without geopolitical overhang — with intra-Brics trade expanding over 13 times in two decades while intra-G-7 trade only doubled, and Brics+ share of world merchandise imports rising from 11% in 2000 to 17% by 2025 as G-7's share fell from 45% to 25%. He writes that Brics must avoid becoming an eloquent talk shop and squandering a unique window to advance multipolarisation, with China and India obliged to cushion the world economy and supply public goods while frontline members like Iran resist aggression, and notes Xi and Modi are demonstrating that India-China differences will not prevent them from steering the multipolar age positively.

- Daulet Singh argues that a decade ago Brics was seen as a supporting actor in US-led order-building, but the US has become a spoiler through weaponisation of public goods, and Brics is now the only multilateral institution upholding interconnections between diverse economies without geopolitical overhang — with intra-Brics trade expanding over 13 times in two decades while intra-G-7 trade only doubled, and Brics+ share of world merchandise imports rising from 11% in 2000 to 17% by 2025 as G-7's share fell from 45% to 25%.
- He writes that Brics must avoid becoming an eloquent talk shop and squandering a unique window to advance multipolarisation, with China and India obliged to cushion the world economy and supply public goods while frontline members like Iran resist aggression, and notes Xi and Modi are demonstrating that India-China differences will not prevent them from steering the multipolar age positively.
_Sources: Times of India Bengaluru p.11_

### China's X factor, and why India needs its own narrative (Opinion)

**In short:** Former ambassador Ashok K Kantha writes that Xi Jinping's 2013 call to "tell China's story well" has spawned a large institutional ecosystem for "discourse power," including well-funded think-tanks and quasi-private bodies, but Chinese transgressions in eastern Ladakh and Galwan in 2020 broke the pattern of India-China Think-Tanks Forum meetings almost overnight. He notes that despite more calibrated outreach — including Chinese scholars building followings on X, advertorials on Tibet, and journalist fellowships — Pew's modest global warming toward China in 2025–2026 barely touched India, where America is still favoured, and argues no amount of nimble messaging will improve how China is seen in India as long as it treats India as a lesser power expected to defer rather than a peer civilisation with its own agency, while India should build its own narrative in China using organic assets such as accounting for at least 10% of China's record trade surplus last year and Buddhism's birthplace status.

- Former ambassador Ashok K Kantha writes that Xi Jinping's 2013 call to "tell China's story well" has spawned a large institutional ecosystem for "discourse power," including well-funded think-tanks and quasi-private bodies, but Chinese transgressions in eastern Ladakh and Galwan in 2020 broke the pattern of India-China Think-Tanks Forum meetings almost overnight.
- He notes that despite more calibrated outreach — including Chinese scholars building followings on X, advertorials on Tibet, and journalist fellowships — Pew's modest global warming toward China in 2025–2026 barely touched India, where America is still favoured, and argues no amount of nimble messaging will improve how China is seen in India as long as it treats India as a lesser power expected to defer rather than a peer civilisation with its own agency, while India should build its own narrative in China using organic assets such as accounting for at least 10% of China's record trade surplus last year and Buddhism's birthplace status.
_Sources: Times of India Bengaluru p.11_

### What the cute orangutans reveal about our 'ugly' biases (Opinion)

**In short:** Environment journalist Bahar Dutt writes that orangutans arriving on India's coastline made national headlines as "SO cute" but are victims of an international crime network, and since Indonesia has formally requested their return India should repatriate them under CITES once origin is confirmed — while noting a UN Environment report found over 70,000 native and exotic live animals and derivatives trafficked through 18 Indian airports between 2011 and 2020, with reptiles accounting for 46% of seizures yet rarely eliciting sympathy. She urges the environment minister to expedite repatriation, customs to explain how live animals entered through India's coast, and consumers to examine palm-oil presence in products, noting ICAR has identified over 133,000 hectares in Arunachal Pradesh as suitable for palm oil cultivation, raising deforestation concerns, and that every reptile or "ugly" animal seized in the wildlife trade also deserves dignity instead of languishing in rescue centres.

- Environment journalist Bahar Dutt writes that orangutans arriving on India's coastline made national headlines as "SO cute" but are victims of an international crime network, and since Indonesia has formally requested their return India should repatriate them under CITES once origin is confirmed — while noting a UN Environment report found over 70,000 native and exotic live animals and derivatives trafficked through 18 Indian airports between 2011 and 2020, with reptiles accounting for 46% of seizures yet rarely eliciting sympathy.

### Houthis may put world in dire straits over diesel (Opinion)

**In short:** Swaminathan S Anklesaria Aiyar writes that Houthis capturing Mocha port and islands controlling Bab el-Mandeb — through which 9.3 mbd of oil and petroleum products passed before 2023 attacks halved flows to 4.6 mbd by 2025 — may create a disastrous shortage of refined products especially diesel, with Brent crude already rising from $90/barrel to $105/barrel and US retail diesel crossing $6/gallon and possibly hitting $8/gallon, double its price at the year's start. He notes Saudi Arabia diverted exports through its East-West pipeline to the Red Sea reaching 8.1 mbd in the April–June quarter after Iran blocked the Strait of Hormuz in April 2026, but Houthis attacking Saudi cities and refineries have now shut down production, Trump twice declined MBS's personal appeals for US military action despite a Nov Strategic Defense Agreement, and if both Hormuz and Bab el-Mandeb remain closed India's oil subsidy bill could go through the roof despite soaring refinery profits as refined product prices outpace crude.

- Swaminathan S Anklesaria Aiyar writes that Houthis capturing Mocha port and islands controlling Bab el-Mandeb — through which 9.3 mbd of oil and petroleum products passed before 2023 attacks halved flows to 4.6 mbd by 2025 — may create a disastrous shortage of refined products especially diesel, with Brent crude already rising from $90/barrel to $105/barrel and US retail diesel crossing $6/gallon and possibly hitting $8/gallon, double its price at the year's start.

### Persons of forest, prisoners of men: How orangutans turn up in India (Opinion)

**In short:** Five baby orangutans seized in Odisha highlight illegal wildlife trade from their native Malaysia and Indonesia range, likely driven by demand for private exotic menageries rather than kinship with humans who share nearly 97% of their DNA. Ecologist Seshadri K S argues the infants were probably snatched from killed mothers, that India’s CITES enforcement through the Wildlife Crime Control Bureau on a Rs 13 crore budget is overstretched, and that the five orphans should be repatriated to semi-wild settings in their home range rather than kept in zoos or private rescue centres.

- Five baby orangutans seized in Odisha highlight illegal wildlife trade from their native Malaysia and Indonesia range, likely driven by demand for private exotic menageries rather than kinship with humans who share nearly 97% of their DNA.

### Police station footage: Seen and unseen (Opinion)

**In short:** Venkatesh Nayak contrasts recent pro-accountability judicial signals with an Allahabad High Court order upholding denial of RTI access to police-station CCTV footage under Section 8(1)(g), limiting release to courts or human rights commissions only after a complaint. He notes mandatory CCTV under the Supreme Court’s 2020 Paramvir Singh Saini directions, Karnataka Information Commission penalties of Rs 15,000-25,000 on three Kalaburagi police officers for non-compliance, and argues footage should not become a “sarkari secret” when citizens lawfully record police interactions.

- Venkatesh Nayak contrasts recent pro-accountability judicial signals with an Allahabad High Court order upholding denial of RTI access to police-station CCTV footage under Section 8(1)(g), limiting release to courts or human rights commissions only after a complaint.

### BRICS raises the reform pitch (Opinion)

**In short:** Srikanth Kondapalli writes that the 18th BRICS Summit’s 140-paragraph New Delhi Declaration pushes multipolarity, UN/UNSC/IMF reform, and Global South leadership while treading cautiously on West Asia and omitting de-dollarisation despite nearly half of $1.1 trillion intra-BRICS trade settling in renminbi. The declaration condemned the 2025 Pahalgam attack that killed 26 people, backed the New Development Bank, and called for reliable critical-mineral supply chains amid tariff and sanctions disruptions.

### Information, truth and connection in the AI age (Opinion)

**In short:** Gopichand Katragadda responds to Yuval Noah Harari by defining information as data with sufficient context to reduce uncertainty for a given purpose, not inherently truth-bearing connection. He warns that generative AI makes fluent falsehood cheap, urges readers to verify primary sources and treat AI answers as leads, and argues societies must distinguish data, belief, and truth rather than confuse political narrative with statistical analysis.

- Gopichand Katragadda responds to Yuval Noah Harari by defining information as data with sufficient context to reduce uncertainty for a given purpose, not inherently truth-bearing connection.
- He warns that generative AI makes fluent falsehood cheap, urges readers to verify primary sources and treat AI answers as leads, and argues societies must distinguish data, belief, and truth rather than confuse political narrative with statistical analysis.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.15_
